.

Note that we introduce
\widehat{\mu}_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{Z}(\cdot)
in addition to
\widehat{\mu}_{{\mathcal{S}}}(\cdot)
. This is because, in the full conformal algorithm, the target sample
Z
is used for both model fitting and construction of the calibration quantile. To emphasize this role and distinguish it from the pretrained-score setting, we adopt a separate notation.

C.1.1
Key lemmas

We first single out two key lemmas that play a pivotal role in the proof of Proposition
4.1
. Here and throughout, we take
L=L_{1}L_{2}
.

The first lemma characterizes the discrepancy between the tail distribution of the scores conditional on a random calibration set and the corresponding tail distribution obtained after averaging over the randomness of the calibration set, provided that a stable learning algorithm is used for model fitting. The proof is deferred to
Section

C.3.1
.

Lemma C.1
.

Consider the same setting as in
Proposition

4.1
. Let
\widehat{\mu}^{(X,Y)}(\cdot)
denote a fitted model trained on the data
Z_{1:n}^{\mathsf{train}}
together with the target sample
Z=(X,Y)
, and assume that
\widehat{\mu}^{(X,Y)}(\cdot)
satisfies Assumption
4.3
with coefficient
L_{2}
. Further, suppose that for each
i\in[m]
,
Z_{i}^{\mathsf{cal}}=(X_{i}^{\mathsf{cal}},Y_{i}^{\mathsf{cal}})
is independently drawn from
{\mathcal{D}}_{i}
, and let the target pair
Z=(X,Y)\sim{\mathcal{D}}
.
Then, for any
\delta\in(0,1)
and conditional on any given realization
Z_{m+1:n}^{\mathsf{train}}=z_{m+1:n}^{\mathsf{train}}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left\{\bigg|{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\left(\big|Y-\widehat{\mu}^{(X,Y)}(X)\big|>x\,\Big|\,Z_{1:m}^{\mathsf{cal}},Z_{m+1:n}^{\mathsf{train}}=z_{m+1:n}^{\mathsf{train}}\right)-{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}\times{\mathcal{D}}}\left(\big|Y-\widehat{\mu}^{(X,Y)}(X)\big|>x\right)\bigg|\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\frac{16{L}}{n}\sqrt{m\log\frac{1}{\delta}}

(84)

with probability at least
1-\delta
. Here, we adopt the notation

{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}\times{\mathcal{D}}}\left(\big|Y-\widehat{\mu}^{(X,Y)}(X)\big|>x\right)\coloneqq{\mathbb{E}}_{Z_{1:m}^{\mathsf{cal}}}\left[{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\left(\big|Y-\widehat{\mu}^{(X,Y)}(X)\big|>x\,\Big|\,Z_{1:m}^{\mathsf{cal}},Z_{m+1:n}^{\mathsf{train}}=z_{m+1:n}^{\mathsf{train}}\right)\right].

Another key lemma establishes a high-probability upper bound on the deviation of the average empirical scores from their mean over a given time window. In contrast to the pretrained-score setting, full conformal methods induce complicated statistical dependency among the scores
\{s_{i}\}
, leading to additional technical difficulties. The proof of this lemma is provided in
Section

C.3.2
.

Lemma C.2
.

Consider the setting same in
Proposition

4.1
. Let

\widehat{\mu}(\cdot)
represent a fitted model trained on
Z_{1:n}^{\mathsf{train}}
satisfying Assumption
4.3
with coefficient
L_{2}
.
Further, suppose that for each
i\in[m]
,
Z_{i}^{\mathsf{cal}}=(X_{i}^{\mathsf{cal}},Y_{i}^{\mathsf{cal}})
is independently drawn from
{\mathcal{D}}_{i}
.
For every
i=1,\ldots,m
, we let

\displaystyle s_{i}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\big|Y_{i}^{\mathsf{cal}}-\widehat{\mu}(X_{i}^{\mathsf{cal}})\big|,\qquad i=1,\ldots,m.

Then, for any
\delta\in(0,1)
and conditional on any realization
Z_{m+1:n}^{\mathsf{train}}=z_{m+1:n}^{\mathsf{train}}
, the following event

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}}\Biggl\{\frac{1}{m}\bigg|\,\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Big(\mathbbm{1}\left\{s_{i}\leq x\right\}

\displaystyle-{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}}\big(s_{i}\leq x\big)\Big)\,\biggr|\Biggr\}\leq\;24\sqrt{\frac{\log(10/\delta)}{m}}+\frac{24L}{n}\sqrt{m\log\left(\frac{10m}{\delta}+n\right)}

happens with probability at least
1-\delta
.

C.1.2
Proof of Proposition
4.1

For notational convenience, we write
Z_{1:m}
in place of
Z_{1:m}^{\mathsf{cal}}
throughout this proof when it is clear from the context.
Fix an auxiliary sample
z_{0}=(x_{0},y_{0})
, which shall be treated as deterministic in the following. We introduce several addition notation:

•

s_{i}\coloneqq s_{Z_{1:m}\cup\{z_{0}\}}(Z_{i})=\bigl|\,Y_{i}-\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}\cup\{z_{0}\}}(X_{i})\,\bigr|
for
i=1,\ldots,m
, and
s_{\mathsf{test}}\coloneqq\bigl|\,Y-\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}\cup\{z_{0}\}}(X)\,\bigr|
, where
(X,Y)
is not used for model fitting;

•

s_{i}^{(X,Y)}\coloneqq s_{Z_{1:m}}^{Z}(Z_{i})=\bigl|\,Y_{i}-\widehat{\mu}^{(X,Y)}_{Z_{1:m}}(X_{i})\,\bigr|
for
i=1,\ldots,m
, and
s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)}\coloneqq\bigl|\,Y-\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X)\,\bigr|
, where
(X,Y)
is used for for model fitting;

•

\widehat{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}\coloneqq\mathsf{Q}_{1-\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{m+1}\Bigl\{\delta\{s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)}\}+\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\delta\{s_{i}^{(X,Y)}\}\Bigr\}\right)
, which indicates the quantile when
(X,Y)
is also used for model fitting;

•

\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}\coloneqq\mathsf{Q}_{1-\alpha}\left(\frac{1}{m+1}\Bigl\{\delta\{s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)}\}+\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\delta\{s_{i}\}\Bigr\}\right)
; note that except for
s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)}
, the remaining scores
\{s_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}
are computed when
(X,Y)
is not used for training;

•

F_{\mathsf{test}}(u;z_{1:m})\coloneqq{\mathbb{P}}_{(X,Y)\sim{\mathcal{D}}}\Bigl(\bigl|\,Y-\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X)\,\bigr|\leq u\Bigr),~F_{\mathsf{test}}(u)\coloneqq{\mathbb{E}}_{Z_{1:m}\sim{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}}[F_{\mathsf{test}}(u;Z_{1:m})]
;

•

F_{i}(u)\coloneqq{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}\times{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}}\bigl(s_{i}^{(X,Y)}\leq u\bigr)
and
F_{i}^{0}(u)\coloneqq{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}}\bigl(s_{i}\leq u\bigr)
for
i=1,\ldots,m
.

Step 1: eliminating the dependence of the fitted model on
(X,Y)
.

The first step of the proof is to examine the effect of removing the dependence of the fitted model
\widehat{\mu}^{(X,Y)}(\cdot)
on the target sample
(X,Y)
.
To be precise, consider the discrepancy between

{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}(Y\in{\mathcal{C}}(X)\mid Z_{1:m})={\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}(s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)}\leq\widehat{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}\mid Z_{1:m})\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}(s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)}\leq\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}).

For the two score sets
\{s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)}\}\cup\{s_{i}^{(X,Y)}\}_{i=1}^{m}
and
\{s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)}\}\cup\{s_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}
, Assumption
4.3
tells us that

\max\limits_{i\in[m]}\bigl\{\bigl|\,s_{i}^{(X,Y)}-s_{i}\,\bigr|\bigr\}\leq\max\limits_{i\in[m]}\bigl\{\bigl|\,\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}}(X_{i})\,\bigr|\bigr\}\leq\frac{L_{2}}{n}.

Then by virtue of
42
, we obtain

\displaystyle\bigl|\,\widehat{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}-\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}\,\bigr|\leq\frac{L_{2}}{n}

(85)

for any
(X,Y)
and
Z_{1:m}
. Combining this with Assumption
4.2
yields

\displaystyle\Bigl|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}(Y\in{\mathcal{C}}(X)\mid Z_{1:m})

\displaystyle-{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\big(s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)}\leq\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}\mid Z_{1:m}\big)\,\Bigr|

(86)

\displaystyle=\Bigl|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\big(s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)}\leq\widehat{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}\mid Z_{1:m}\big)-{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\big(s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)}\leq\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}\mid Z_{1:m}\big)\,\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\Big(s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)}\in\big[\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}-\bigl|\,\widehat{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}-\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}\,\bigr|,\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}+\bigl|\,\widehat{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}-\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}\,\bigr|\big]\mid Z_{1:m}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\Big(s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)}\in\Big[\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}-\frac{L_{2}}{n},\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}+\frac{L_{2}}{n}\Big]\,\Big|\,Z_{1:m}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4L_{1}L_{2}}{n}=\frac{4L}{n},

where the penultimate line results from (
85
), and the last line is due to Assumption
4.2
. This inequality allows us to switch attention to
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}(s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)}\leq\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}\mid Z_{1:m})
.

Step 2: replacing
\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}
with an adjusted quantile independent of
(X,Y)
.

Note that by definition,
\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}
still depends on the test score
s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)}
, which motivates us to consider replacing
\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}
with an alternative quantile independent of
s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)}
. More precisely, define the following adjusted quantile

\displaystyle\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}\;\coloneqq\;\inf\Big\{x\in{\mathbb{R}}:\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{m}\mathbbm{1}\{s_{i}\leq x\}\geq\lceil(1-\alpha)m-\alpha\rceil\Big\}.,

(87)

which satisfies the following property.

Claim C.1
.

The adjusted quantile defined in (
87
) satisfies

\bigl\{s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(x,y)}\leq\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}\bigr\}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\bigl\{s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(x,y)}\leq\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}\bigr\}.

It then follows immediately from Claim
C.1
that

{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\big(s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)}\leq\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}\mid Z_{1:m}\big)={\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\big(s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)}\leq\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}\mid Z_{1:m}\big)=F_{\mathsf{test}}(\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha};Z_{1:m}),

(88)

where
F_{\mathsf{test}}(\cdot;\cdot)
is defined at the beginning of this subsection. It then boils down to controlling
F_{\mathsf{test}}(\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha};Z_{1:m})
.

Proof of Claim
C.1
.

From the definition of the quantile functional, one has

\displaystyle\bigl\{s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(x,y)}\leq\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}\bigr\}\quad

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\quad s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(x,y)}\leq\inf\Bigl\{q\in\mathbb{R}:\;\frac{1}{m+1}\mathbbm{1}\{s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(x,y)}\leq q\}+\frac{1}{m+1}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbbm{1}\{s_{i}\leq q\}\geq 1-\alpha\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\quad\mathbbm{1}\{s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(x,y)}\leq s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(x,y)}\}+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbbm{1}\{s_{i}\leq s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(x,y)}\}\leq\lceil(1-\alpha)(m+1)\rceil.

Given the trivial fact
\mathbbm{1}\{s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(x,y)}\leq s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(x,y)}\}=1
, the last display is equivalent to

\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbbm{1}\{s_{i}\leq s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(x,y)}\}\leq\lceil(1-\alpha)(m+1)-1\rceil=\lceil(1-\alpha)m-\alpha\rceil.

By the definition (
87
) of
\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}
, this inequality holds if and only if

s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(x,y)}\leq\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}
.
This proves the claim.
∎

Step 3: controlling
F_{\mathsf{test}}(\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha};Z_{1:m})
.

In order to control
F_{\mathsf{test}}(\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha};Z_{1:m})
, we begin with the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\Big|F_{\mathsf{test}}(\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha};Z_{1:m}){-}(1-\alpha)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\left|{F_{\mathsf{test}}(\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha};Z_{1:m})-F_{\mathsf{test}}(\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha})}\right|}_{\eqqcolon{\mathcal{T}}_{1}}{+}\underbrace{\left|{\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\left[F_{i}^{0}(\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha})-\mathbbm{1}\left\{s_{i}\leq\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}\right\}\right]}\right|}_{\eqqcolon{\mathcal{T}}_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\left|{F_{\mathsf{test}}(\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha})-\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}F_{i}^{0}(\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha})}\right|}_{\eqqcolon{\mathcal{T}}_{3}}+\underbrace{\left|{\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\mathbbm{1}\left\{s_{i}\leq\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}\right\}-(1-\alpha)}\right|}_{\eqqcolon{\mathcal{T}}_{4}},

(89)

where both
F_{\mathsf{test}}(\cdot)
and
F_{i}^{0}(\cdot)
are defined at the beginning of this subsection. This decomposition leaves us with four terms to cope with.

•

Bounding
\mathcal{T}_{1}
and
\mathcal{T}_{2}
.

Define the typical events
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
as:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Bigg\{\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\bigg\{\Big|F_{\mathsf{test}}(u;Z_{1:m})-F_{\mathsf{test}}(u)\Big|\bigg\}\leq\frac{16{L}}{n}\sqrt{m\log\frac{2}{\delta}}\Bigg\};

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Bigg\{\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\bigg\{\Big|\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\Big(\mathbbm{1}\left\{s_{i}\leq u\right\}-F_{i}^{0}(u)\Big)\Big|\bigg\}\leq 24\sqrt{\frac{\log(40/\delta)}{m}}+\frac{24{L}}{n}\sqrt{m\log\left(\frac{40m}{\delta}+n\right)}\Bigg\}.

Lemmas
C.1
and
C.2
imply that, with probability at least
1-\delta
, these two events occur simultaneously.
On the event
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
, the terms
{\mathcal{T}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{T}}_{2}
satisfy

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{1}

\displaystyle=\left|{F_{\mathsf{test}}(\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha};Z_{1:m})-F_{\mathsf{test}}(\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha})}\right|\leq\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\Big|\,F_{\mathsf{test}}(u;Z_{1:m})-F_{\mathsf{test}}(u)\,\Big|\leq\frac{16{L}\sqrt{m\log(2/\delta)}}{n};

(90)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{2}

\displaystyle=\left|{\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\left[F_{i}^{0}(\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha})-\mathbbm{1}\left\{s_{i}\leq\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}\right\}\right]}\right|\leq\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left|{\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\left[F_{i}^{0}(u)-\mathbbm{1}\left\{s_{i}\leq u\right\}\right]}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 24\sqrt{\frac{\log(40/\delta)}{m}}+\frac{24{L}}{n}\sqrt{m\log\left(\frac{40m}{\delta}+n\right)}.

(91)

•

Bounding
\mathcal{T}_{3}
.

Regarding
{\mathcal{T}}_{3}
, it follows from the triangle inequality and the definition of the total-variation distance that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{3}

\displaystyle=\left|{F_{\mathsf{test}}(\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha})-\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}F_{i}^{0}(\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha})}\right|\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\left|{F_{\mathsf{test}}(\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha})-F_{i}^{0}(\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha})}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\Big(\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\big\{\left|{F_{\mathsf{test}}(u)-F_{i}(u)}\right|\big\}+\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\big\{\left|{F_{i}^{0}(u)-F_{i}(u)}\right|\big\}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(a)}}{\leq}\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\Big(\mathsf{KS}\big(s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)},s_{i}^{(X,Y)}\big)+\frac{4L}{n}\Big)\overset{\mathrm{(b)}}{\leq}\frac{2}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={0}}^{m}\mathsf{TV}(Z,Z_{i})+\frac{8L}{n},

(92)

where
F_{i}(\cdot)
is defined at the beginning of this subsection. Inequalities (a) and (b) are justified below.

–

To validate inequality (a) in (
92
), observe that

\displaystyle F_{i}^{0}(u)-F_{i}(u)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}\big(s_{i}^{(X,Y)}\leq u\big)-{\mathbb{P}}(s_{i}\leq u)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\leq}{\mathbb{P}}\Big(u-\big|\,\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}\cup\{z_{0}\}}(X_{i})\,\big|<s_{i}\leq u+\big|\,\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}\cup\{z_{0}\}}(X_{i})\,\big|\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{\leq}{\mathbb{P}}\Big(u-\frac{L_{2}}{n}<s_{i}\leq u+\frac{L_{2}}{n}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\big(\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}\cup\{z_{0}\}}(X_{i})-u\big)-\frac{L_{2}}{n}<Y_{i}\leq\big(\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}\cup\{z_{0}\}}(X_{i})-u\big)+\frac{L_{2}}{n}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\big(\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}\cup\{z_{0}\}}(X_{i})+u\big)-\frac{L_{2}}{n}<Y_{i}\leq\big(\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}\cup\{z_{0}\}}(X_{i})+u\big)+\frac{L_{2}}{n}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(iii)}}{\leq}\frac{4L_{1}L_{2}}{n}=\frac{4L}{n},

where (ii) arises from Assumption
4.3
, (iii) follows from Assumption
4.2
, respectively, and (i) is a direct consequence of the definition of
s_{i}^{(X,Y)}
and
s_{i}
(see the beginning of the subsection) and
the following elementary fact.

Fact C.1
.

\big|{\mathbb{P}}(\left|{a}\right|>u)-{\mathbb{P}}(\left|{a+\delta}\right|>u)\big|\leq{\mathbb{P}}(u-\left|{\delta}\right|\leq\left|{a}\right|\leq u+\left|{\delta}\right|).

Proof of Fact
C.1
.

We observe that

\displaystyle\big|{\mathbb{P}}(\left|{a}\right|>u)-{\mathbb{P}}(\left|{a+\delta}\right|>u)\big|

\displaystyle=\max\Big\{{\mathbb{P}}(\left|{a}\right|>u)-{\mathbb{P}}(\left|{a+\delta}\right|>u),\,{\mathbb{P}}(\left|{a+\delta}\right|>u)-{\mathbb{P}}(\left|{a}\right|>u)\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}(\left|{a}\right|+\left|{\delta}\right|>u)-{\mathbb{P}}(\left|{a}\right|-\left|{\delta}\right|>u)\leq{\mathbb{P}}(u-\left|{\delta}\right|\leq\left|{a}\right|\leq u+\left|{\delta}\right|)

as claimed.
∎

–

We now justify inequality (b) in (
92
). Consider any index
i
, and define
{Z}_{1:m}^{i}
as the dataset obtained from
Z_{1:m}
by replacing the
i
-th sample
Z_{i}
with the target sample
Z
. Based on
{Z}_{1:m}^{i}
, introduce the auxiliary score

{s}_{i}^{\prime}\coloneqq\big|\,Y_{i}-\widehat{\mu}_{{Z}_{1:m}^{i}}^{(X_{i},Y_{i})}(X_{i})\,\big|.

In view of Assumption
4.3
,
s_{i}^{\prime}
differs from
s_{i}^{(X,Y)}
by at most
2L_{2}/n
. Combining this with Assumption
4.2
immediately yields

\mathsf{KS}\big(s_{i}^{\prime},s_{i}^{(X,Y)}\big)\leq\frac{4L_{1}L_{2}}{n}=\frac{4L}{n}.

As a consequence, for each
i=1,\ldots,m
we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{KS}\big(s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)},s_{i}^{(X,Y)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathsf{KS}\big(s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)},s_{i}^{\prime}\big)+\mathsf{KS}\big(s_{i}^{\prime},s_{i}^{(X,Y)}\big)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(iv)}}{\leq}\mathsf{TV}\Big(\big(Z_{1:m},Z\big),\big(Z_{1:m}^{i},Z_{i}\big)\Big)+\frac{4L}{n}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{v})}{\leq}2\mathsf{TV}(Z,Z_{i})+\frac{4L}{n}.

Here, (iv) is valid since
s_{\mathsf{test}}^{(X,Y)}
and
s_{i}^{\prime}
are outputs of the same measurable mapping, evaluated at
(Z_{1:m},Z)
and
(Z_{1:m}^{i},Z_{i})
, respectively, whereas (v) invokes
14
.

•

Bounding
\mathcal{T}_{4}
.

According to the definition of
\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}
, the term
{\mathcal{T}}_{4}
can be bounded by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{4}

\displaystyle=\left|{\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\mathbbm{1}\left\{s_{i}\leq\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha}\right\}-(1-\alpha)}\right|=\left|{\frac{\lceil(1-\alpha)m-\alpha\rceil}{m}-\left(1-\alpha\right)}\right|<\frac{1}{m},

(93)

where the last inequality follows since

\displaystyle-1<{(1-\alpha)m-\alpha}-(1-\alpha)m

\displaystyle\leq{\lceil(1-\alpha)m-\alpha\rceil}-(1-\alpha)m

\displaystyle\leq(1-\alpha)m-\alpha+1-(1-\alpha)m<1.

Substituting the preceding bounds on
\mathcal{T}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{T}_{4}
into (
89
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\Big|F_{\mathsf{test}}(\check{\mathsf{Q}}_{1-\alpha};Z_{1:m}){-}(1-\alpha)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16{L}\sqrt{m\log(2/\delta)}}{n}+24\sqrt{\frac{\log(40/\delta)}{m}}+\frac{24{L}}{n}\sqrt{m\log\left(\frac{40m}{\delta}+n\right)}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={0}}^{m}\mathsf{TV}(Z,Z_{i})+\frac{8L}{n}+\frac{1}{m}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{40L\sqrt{m\log(45n/\delta)}}{n}+24\sqrt{\frac{\log(40/\delta)}{m}}+\frac{2}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={0}}^{m}\mathsf{TV}(Z,Z_{i})+\frac{8L}{n}+\frac{1}{m}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{48L\sqrt{m\log(45n/\delta)}}{n}+25\sqrt{\frac{\log(40/\delta)}{m}}+\frac{2}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={0}}^{m}\mathsf{TV}(Z,Z_{i}).

(94)

Step 4: putting all this together.

Finally, taking
the above results (
86
), (
88
) and (
94
) collectively, we can readily finish the proof of Proposition
4.1
.

C.2
Proof of Theorem
4.1

This section is dedicated to establishing Theorem
4.1
.

Notation.

For ease of presentation, we adopt the notation introduced in
Definition

B.1
as well as in
Section

4.1
. In addition, we shall adopt the following notation:

•

For any
k\leq m<t\in[T]
, let

\displaystyle\mathsf{Q}_{1-\alpha}^{k,m,t}\coloneqq\mathsf{Q}_{1-\alpha}\!\left(\frac{1}{m-k+2}\Bigl(\delta\{s^{Z_{t}}_{Z_{1:m}}(Z_{t})\}+\sum_{l=k}^{m}\delta\{s^{Z_{t}}_{Z_{1:m}}(Z_{l})\}\Bigr)\right),

(95)

representing the quantile when (i) the data
Z_{1:m}\cup\{Z_{t}\}
are used for training; and (ii) the data
Z_{k:m}\cup\{Z_{t}\}
are used for calibration.

•

For any
k\leq m<i\leq j
, define the event

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}(k,m;i,j)\coloneqq\Bigg\{\bigg|\sum_{t=i}^{j}\Bigl(\mathbbm{1}\big\{s^{Z_{t}}_{Z_{1:m}}(Z_{t})\leq\mathsf{Q}_{1-\alpha}^{k,m,t}\big\}-{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{t}}\big(s^{Z_{t}}_{Z_{1:m}}(Z_{t})\leq\mathsf{Q}_{1-\alpha}^{k,m,t}\mid Z_{1:m}\big)\Bigr)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{(j-i+1)\log(2j)}\Bigg\},

(96a)

which is concerned with the deviation of the empirical coverage from the training-conditional mean coverage over the time window
[i,j]
. As we shall see momentarily, this is a high-probability event.

•

For any stage-round pair
(n,r)
, take

{\mathcal{A}}_{n,r}\coloneqq\bigcap_{i=\tau_{n,r}}^{\tau_{n,r+1}-1}\ \bigcap_{j=i+1}^{\tau_{n,r+1}-1}{\mathcal{A}}(\tau_{n,r-1},\tau_{n,r}-1;i,j).

(96b)

Step 1: regret decomposition.

Following the proof structure of
Theorem

3.1
, we decompose the cumulative regret of interest as

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{{t}={1}}^{T}\big|{\mathbb{P}}(Y_{t}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{t}(X_{t})\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{t})-(1-\alpha)\big|

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\sum\limits_{{r}={1}}^{r_{n}}\sum\limits_{{l}={1}}^{T_{r}}\Big|{\mathbb{P}}(Y_{n,r,l}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(X_{n,r,l})\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r})-(1-\alpha)\Big|\,\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{A}}_{n,r}\}

(97)

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\sum\limits_{{r}={1}}^{r_{n}}\sum\limits_{{l}={1}}^{T_{r}}\Big|{\mathbb{P}}(Y_{n,r,l}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(X_{n,r,l})\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r})-(1-\alpha)\Big|\,\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{A}}_{n,r}^{\mathrm{c}}\}.

Here we write
{\mathbb{P}}(\,\cdot\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r})
(or
{\mathbb{P}}(\,\cdot\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{t})
) for the conditional probability given the set-valued mapping
{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(\cdot)
(or
{\mathcal{C}}_{t}(\cdot)
).
Further, similar to (
39
), we augment the data to simplify notation: although the last round of stage
n
contains only
t_{n}\leq T_{r}
time instances, we still generate
Z_{n,r,l}=(X_{n,r,l},Y_{n,r,l})
for every
l>t_{n}
in an i.i.d. manner obeying

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(Y_{n,r,l}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(X_{n,r,l})\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r})=1-\alpha\qquad\text{for all }l>t_{n}.

(98)

Step 2: bounding the second term on the right-hand side of (
97
).

Akin to the pretrained-score setting, the first term on the right-hand side of (
97
) is the dominant term in the above regret decomposition. To justify this, let us look at the second term on the right-hand side of (
97
).
Fix a realization
Z_{1:m}=z_{1:m}
. Then for each
t\in[i,j]
with
m<i
, the indicator

\mathbbm{1}\!\big\{s_{z_{1:m}}^{Z_{t}}(Z_{t})\leq Q_{1-\alpha}^{k,m,t}\big\}
is a function of
Z_{t}
only, and hence this collection of indicator variables over the time window
[i,j]
are statistically independent.
Moreover, the conditional mean of this indicator variable at time
t
is

{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{t}}\!\big(s_{z_{1:m}}^{Z_{t}}(Z_{t})\leq Q_{1-\alpha}^{k,m,t}\mid Z_{1:m}=z_{1:m}\big)
.
Therefore, Hoeffding’s inequality readily yields

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\big({\mathcal{A}}(k,m;i,j)^{\mathrm{c}}\mid Z_{1:m}=z_{1:m}\big)\leq j^{-8}\quad\text{for all }z_{1:m}

(99a)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\qquad

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\big({\mathcal{A}}(k,m;i,j)^{\mathrm{c}}\big)\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{Z_{1:m}}\big[{\mathbb{P}}\big({\mathcal{A}}(k,m;i,j)^{\mathrm{c}}\mid Z_{1:m}\big)\big]\leq j^{-8}.

(99b)

Now consider any time point
t\geq 4
that resides within round
r
of stage
n
. By the construction of
DriftOCP
-
full
, we have

\frac{t}{16}\leq\frac{\tau_{n,r}}{4}\leq\tau_{n,r-1}<\tau_{n,r}\leq t\leq\tau_{n,r+1}\leq 4t.

Consequently, defining

{\mathcal{E}}_{t}\coloneqq\bigcap\limits_{m=\frac{t}{4}}^{t}\bigcap\limits_{k=\frac{t}{16}}^{m}\bigcap\limits_{j=\frac{t}{4}}^{4t}\bigcap\limits_{i=\frac{t}{4}}^{t}{\mathcal{A}}(k,m;i,j)

in which the index pair
(k,m)
ranges over all values that

(\tau_{n,r-1},\tau_{n,r})
may take,
we have

{\mathcal{E}}_{t}\subseteq{\mathcal{A}}_{n,r}.

Regarding this event
\mathcal{E}_{t}
, it follows from (
99
) that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}}_{t}^{\mathrm{c}})

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{{m}={\frac{t}{4}}}^{t}\sum\limits_{{k}={\frac{t}{16}}}^{m}\sum\limits_{{j}={\frac{t}{4}}}^{4t}\sum\limits_{{i}={\frac{t}{4}}}^{j}{\mathbb{P}}\big({\mathcal{A}}(k,m;i,j)^{\mathrm{c}}\big)\leq t^{2}\sum\limits_{{j}={\frac{t}{4}}}^{4t}j{\mathbb{P}}\big({\mathcal{A}}(k,m;i,j)^{\mathrm{c}}\big)\leq t^{2}\sum\limits_{{j}={\frac{t}{4}}}^{4t}\frac{1}{j^{7}}=O\big(t^{-4}\big).

Therefore, the second term on the right-hand side of (
97
) can be bounded above by

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\sum\limits_{{r}={1}}^{r_{n}}\sum\limits_{{l}={1}}^{T_{r}}

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathbb{P}}(Y_{n,r,l}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(X_{n,r,l})\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r})-(1-\alpha)\Big|\,\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{A}}_{n,r}^{\mathrm{c}}\}\bigg]

(100)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum\limits_{{t}={1}}^{T}\big|{\mathbb{P}}(Y_{t}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{t}(X_{t})\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r})-\alpha\big|\,\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{E}}_{t}^{\mathrm{c}}\}\right]\leq\sum\limits_{{t}={1}}^{T}{\mathbb{P}}\big({\mathcal{E}}_{t}^{\mathrm{c}}\big)=O\left(\sum\limits_{{t}={1}}^{T}\frac{1}{t^{4}}\right)=O(1).

Step 3: bounding the first term on the right-hand side of (
97
).

It remains to bound the first term on the right-hand side of (
97
).
The overall proof follows a similar strategy to that used in the pretrained-score setting.
To avoid unnecessary repetition, we shall focus primarily on the steps that differ nontrivially from the pretrained-score case.

To begin with,
by adapting the arguments in the proof of Lemma
B.2
, we obtain the following result, whose proof of
Lemma

C.3
is deferred to
Section

C.3.3
.

Lemma C.3
.

Consider any stage
n
in Algorithm
4
, which comprises
r_{n}
rounds and
S_{n}
time points.
Reusing the notation introduced in
Definition

B.1
, we have

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{{r}={1}}^{r_{n}}\bigg(\sum\limits_{{l}={1}}^{T_{r}}\Big|{\mathbb{P}}(Y_{n,r,l}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r})-(1-\alpha)\Big|\bigg)\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{A}}_{n,r}\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\begin{cases}\widetilde{O}\bigg(\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{J_{n}}\sqrt{\left|{{\mathcal{I}}_{n,j}}\right|}\bigg),~&\text{for the change-point setting},\\
\widetilde{O}\left(\sqrt{S_{n}}+(\mathsf{TV}_{n}^{\mathsf{stage}})^{\frac{1}{3}}S_{n}^{\frac{2}{3}}\right),~&\text{for the smooth drift setting},\end{cases}

(101)

where
we set

\mathsf{TV}_{n}^{\mathsf{stage}}\coloneqq\sum_{r=1}^{r_{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{S_{n,r}-1}\mathsf{TV}(Z_{n,r,l},Z_{n,r,l+1}).

(102)

Given that
Lemma

C.3
controls the cumulative regret within a single stage, it remains to extend this bound to the entire time horizon, which we handle separately for the two drift settings in Steps 4 and 5.

Before proceeding, let us introduce a collection of typical events that will be used in both settings.
For any two time points
1\leq k<m\leq T
, define the event

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}(k,m)\coloneqq\bigg\{\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(Y_{m}\notin{\mathcal{C}}(X_{m}

\displaystyle\mid Z_{k:m-1},Z_{1:m-1})\mid Z_{1:m-1}\Big)-\alpha\,\Big|\leq 2^{6}\sqrt{\frac{\log(40m)}{m-k}}

\displaystyle+\frac{2^{7}L\sqrt{(m-k)\log(40m)}}{m}+\frac{1}{m-k}\sum\limits_{{l}={k}}^{m-1}\mathsf{TV}\big(Z_{m},Z_{l}\big)\bigg\},

(103)

as motivated by
Proposition

4.1
. Here the notation
{\mathcal{C}}(\cdot\mid\cdot,\cdot)
is defined at the beginning of
Appendix

C
.
For any stage
n
, define the typical event
{\mathcal{B}}_{n}
as follows

{\mathcal{B}}_{n}\coloneqq{\mathcal{A}}_{n,r_{n}}\cap{\mathcal{G}}(\tau_{n,r_{n}-1},\tau_{n,r_{n}}).

(104)

In addition, the following two lemmas will be used in the analysis for both drift settings, and we therefore state them here for subsequent use. The first lemma shows that
\mathcal{B}_{n}
is an event with sufficiently high probability (even when suitably weighted by
\tau_{n+1}
); the proof can be found in
Section

C.3.4
.

Lemma C.4
.

For any
n\geq 1
,
recall that
\tau_{n}
denotes the starting time of stage
n
. Then we have

{\mathbb{E}}\big[\tau_{n+1}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}^{\mathrm{c}}\}\big]\leq O\big(n^{-2}\big).

Another useful lemma shows that the aggregate total variation over the last two rounds of each stage is sufficiently large (at least on some high-probability event). The proof can be found in
Section

C.3.5
.

Lemma C.5
.

For any stage
n\leq N-1
, define

\mathsf{TV}_{n}^{\mathsf{tail}}\coloneqq\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{T_{r_{n}}-1}\mathsf{TV}(Z_{n,r_{n},j},Z_{n,r_{n},j{+}1})+\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{T_{r_{n}{-}1}}\mathsf{TV}(Z_{n,r_{n}-1,i},Z_{n,r_{n}{-}1,i+1}),

(105)

and introduce the event

{\mathcal{H}}_{n}\coloneqq\Big\{T_{r_{n}-1}\sqrt{\log(40\tau_{n,r_{n}})}\leq\frac{\tau_{n,r_{n}}}{256}\Big\}.

(106)

Recalling that
t_{n}\leq T_{r_{n}}
is the number of iterations in round
r_{n}
of stage
n
,
one has

\sqrt{t_{n}}\,\mathsf{TV}_{n}^{\mathsf{tail}}\mathbbm{1}\big\{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}\cap{\mathcal{H}}_{n}\big\}\geq 3\cdot\mathbbm{1}\big\{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}\cap{\mathcal{H}}_{n}\big\}.

Step 4: analysis for the change-point setting.

In this setting,

Lemma

C.3
allows one to decompose

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{r=1}^{r_{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{T_{r}}

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}(Y_{n,r,l}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(X_{n,r,l})\,|\,{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r})-\alpha\big|\,\mathbbm{1}\{\mathcal{A}_{n,r}\}\leq\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\sum_{n=1}^{N}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{J_{n}}\sqrt{|\mathcal{I}_{n,j}|}\bigg)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\sum_{n=1}^{N}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{J_{n}}\sqrt{|\mathcal{I}_{n,j}|}\bigg)\,\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}\}+\sum_{n=1}^{N}(\tau_{n+1}-\tau_{n})\,\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}^{\mathrm{c}}\}\Bigg),

(107)

where the last line follows since, by Cauchy-Schwarz,

\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{J_{n}}\sqrt{\big|\,{\mathcal{I}}_{n,j}\,\big|}\leq\sqrt{J_{n}\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{J_{n}}\big|\,{\mathcal{I}}_{n,j}\,\big|}=\sqrt{J_{n}(\tau_{n+1}-\tau_{n})}\leq\tau_{n+1}-\tau_{n}.

•

We start with the last term on the right-hand side of (
107
), for which
Lemma

C.4
indicates that

{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}(\tau_{n+1}-\tau_{n})\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}^{\mathrm{c}}\}\bigg]\leq\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{\infty}{\mathbb{E}}\big[\tau_{n+1}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}^{\mathrm{c}}\}\big]\leq O\left(\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right)=O(1).

(108)

•

When it comes to the first term on the right-hand side of (
107
), we first divide it into

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{N}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{J_{n}}\sqrt{|\mathcal{I}_{n,j}|}\bigg)\,\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\bigg(\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{J_{n}-1}\sqrt{|{\mathcal{I}}_{n,j}|}\bigg)

(109)

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\sqrt{|{\mathcal{I}}_{n,J_{n}}|}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}\cap{\mathcal{H}}_{n}\}+\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\sqrt{|{\mathcal{I}}_{n,J_{n}}|}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}^{\mathrm{c}}\},

where
{\mathcal{H}}_{n}
is defined in (
106
).
We shall bound the three terms on the right-hand side of (
109
) separately.

–

As for the first term on the right-hand side of (
109
), we first make the observation that: the time segments
{\mathcal{I}}_{n,j}
(
n=1,\ldots,N
and
j=1,\ldots,J_{n}-1
) belong to distinct time segments in
\{{\mathcal{I}}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{N^{\mathsf{cp}}+1}
. As a result, we can derive

\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{J_{n}-1}\sqrt{|{\mathcal{I}}_{n,j}|}\leq\sum\limits_{{k}={1}}^{N^{\mathsf{cp}}+1}\sqrt{|{\mathcal{I}}_{k}|}\leq\sqrt{(N^{\mathsf{cp}}+1)T},

(110)

where the last relation arises from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.

–

With regards to the second term on the right-hand side of (
109
), by the construction of
{\mathcal{I}}_{n,j}
we know that, for each terminal interval
{\mathcal{I}}_{n,J_{n}}
, there exists a unique time segment
{\mathcal{I}}_{k_{n}}
defined in
Definition

B.1
such that
{\mathcal{I}}_{n,J_{n}}\subseteq{\mathcal{I}}_{k_{n}}
. Moreover, the indices are nondecreasing, namely
k_{n}\leq k_{n+1}
for
n=1,\ldots,N-1
. In particular, when
J_{n}\geq 2
, stage
n
must contain a distribution change, which implies
k_{n}>k_{n-1}
. Using these properties, we can obtain

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\sqrt{|{\mathcal{I}}_{n,J_{n}}|}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}\cap{\mathcal{H}}_{n}\}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(a)}}{\leq}\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\sqrt{|{\mathcal{I}}_{n,J_{n}}|}\mathbbm{1}\{J_{n}\geq 2\}

(111)

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\sqrt{\big|\,{\mathcal{I}}_{k_{n}}\,\big|}\mathbbm{1}\{k_{n}>k_{n-1}\}\leq\sum\limits_{{k}={1}}^{N^{\mathsf{cp}}+1}\sqrt{|{\mathcal{I}}_{k}|}\leq\sqrt{(N^{\mathsf{cp}}+1)T}.

Here, (a) follows since, on the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{n}\cap{\mathcal{H}}_{n}
, we have
\sqrt{t_{n}}\mathsf{TV}_{n}^{\mathsf{tail}}\geq 3>0
(according to
Lemma

C.5
), which implies that stage
n
must contain a distribution shift and hence necessarily requires
J_{n}\geq 2
.

–

It remains to bound the third term on the right-hand side of (
109
). To this end, we make note of the following relations between the two sequences
\{\tau_{n,r}\}_{n,r}
and
\{T_{r}\}_{r}
:

\displaystyle\tau_{n-1,r_{n-1}}\leq\tau_{n,r_{n}-1};\quad\tau_{n,r_{n}}-\tau_{n,r_{n}-1}=T_{r_{n}-1}.

In particular, the intervals
\{[\tau_{n,r_{n}-1}+1,\tau_{n,r_{n}}]\}_{n=1}^{N}
are pairwise disjoint, and as a result,

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}^{\mathrm{c}}\}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\mathbbm{1}\left\{{T_{r_{n}-1}\sqrt{\log(40\tau_{n,r_{n}})}}>\frac{\tau_{n,r_{n}}}{256{L}}\right\}\leq\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\frac{256{L}\sqrt{\log(40T)}(\tau_{n,r_{n}}-\tau_{n,r_{n}-1})}{\tau_{n,r_{n}}}

\displaystyle\leq 256{L}\sqrt{\log(40T)}\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\sum\limits_{{i}={\tau_{n,r_{n}-1}+1}}^{\tau_{n,r_{n}}}\frac{1}{\tau_{n,r_{n}}}\leq 256{L}\sqrt{\log(40T)}\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\sum\limits_{{i}={\tau_{n,r_{n}-1}+1}}^{\tau_{n,r_{n}}}\frac{1}{i}

\displaystyle\leq 256{L}\sqrt{\log(40T)}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{T}\frac{1}{i}\leq 256{L}\big(\log(40T)\big)^{\frac{3}{2}}.

(112)

Consequently, taking this together with the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality yields

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\sqrt{|{\mathcal{I}}_{n,J_{n}}|}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}^{\mathrm{c}}\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\sqrt{S_{n}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}^{\mathrm{c}}\right\}{\leq}\sqrt{\left(\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}S_{n}\right)\left(\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}^{\mathrm{c}}\}\right)}

(113)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{T\left(\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}^{\mathrm{c}}\}\right)}\overset{\eqref{eq:full_conf_B_n_bound_2}}{\leq}\widetilde{O}\big(\sqrt{LT}\big).

Combining (
107
)–(
111
) and (
113
) reveals that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{r=1}^{r_{n}}\sum_{l=1}^{T_{r}}

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{P}(Y_{n,r,l}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(X_{n,r,l})\,|\,{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r})-\alpha\big|\,\mathbbm{1}\{\mathcal{A}_{n,r}\}\Bigg]\leq\widetilde{O}(\sqrt{(N^{\mathsf{cp}}+L+1)T}),

(114)

which together with
(
97
) and (
100
)
establishes the advertised regret bound for the change-point setting.

Step 5: analysis for the smooth drift setting.

With
Lemma

C.3
in mind, we first analyze

\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sqrt{S_{n}}
.
Recalling the definition of
\mathsf{TV}_{n}^{\mathsf{tail}}
in (
105
), we make the observation that

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\sqrt{S_{n}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{S_{N}}+\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N-1}\sqrt{S_{n}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}\right\}\mathbbm{1}\left\{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}\right\}+\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\sqrt{S_{n}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}^{\mathrm{c}}\right\}\mathbbm{1}\left\{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}\right\}

(115)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{T}+\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N-1}\sqrt{S_{n}}\left(\sqrt{t_{n}}\mathsf{TV}_{n}^{\mathsf{tail}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}+\sqrt{\left(\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}S_{n}\right)\left(\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}^{\mathrm{c}}\}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{T}+\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N-1}S_{n}^{\frac{2}{3}}\big(\mathsf{TV}_{n}^{\mathsf{tail}}\big)^{\frac{1}{3}}+\sqrt{T\left(\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}^{\mathrm{c}}\}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{T}+\left(\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N-1}S_{n}\right)^{\frac{2}{3}}\left(\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N-1}\mathsf{TV}_{n}^{\mathsf{tail}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}+\sqrt{T\left(\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}^{\mathrm{c}}\}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{T}+2T^{\frac{2}{3}}\mathsf{TV}_{T}^{\frac{1}{3}}+\sqrt{T\left(\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}^{\mathrm{c}}\}\right)}\overset{\eqref{eq:full_conf_B_n_bound_2}}{=}\widetilde{O}\Big(T^{\frac{2}{3}}\mathsf{TV}_{T}^{\frac{1}{3}}+\sqrt{(L+1)T}\Big),

where the second line arises from
Lemma

C.5
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the penultimate line results from Hölder’s inequality, and the last inequality holds because for any
n
,
\mathsf{TV}_{n}^{\mathsf{tail}}
is counted at most twice in the summation from
1
to
N
. Taking this collectively with
Lemma

C.4
, we can demonstrate that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\sqrt{S_{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\sqrt{S_{n}}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}\}\right]+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\sqrt{S_{n}}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}^{\mathrm{c}}\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}\left(\sqrt{(L+1)T}+T^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathsf{TV}_{T}^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\tau_{n+1}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}^{c}\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}\left(\sqrt{(L+1)T}+T^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathsf{TV}_{T}^{\frac{1}{3}}\right)+\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{\infty}{\mathbb{E}}\big[\tau_{n+1}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}^{c}\}\big]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{O}\left(\sqrt{(L+1)T}+T^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathsf{TV}_{T}^{\frac{1}{3}}+\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right)=\widetilde{O}\left(\sqrt{(L+1)T}+\mathsf{TV}_{T}^{\frac{1}{3}}T^{\frac{2}{3}}\right).

Armed with the above bound, we can readily invoke
Lemma

C.3
and apply Hölder’s inequality to yield

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\sum\limits_{{r}={1}}^{r_{n}}\sum\limits_{{l}={1}}^{T_{r}}\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}(Y_{n,r,l}\in

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(X_{n,r,l})\,|\,{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r})-(1-\alpha)\,\Big|\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{A}}_{n,r}\}\Bigg]=\widetilde{O}\bigg({\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\sqrt{S_{n}}\bigg]+\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\big(\mathsf{TV}_{n}^{\mathsf{stage}}\big)^{\frac{1}{3}}S_{n}^{\frac{2}{3}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\sqrt{(L+1)T}+\mathsf{TV}_{T}^{\frac{1}{3}}T^{\frac{2}{3}}+\bigg(\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}\mathsf{TV}_{n}^{\mathsf{stage}}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{3}}\bigg(\sum\limits_{{n}={1}}^{N}S_{n}\bigg)^{\frac{2}{3}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}\Big(\sqrt{(L+1)T}+\mathsf{TV}_{T}^{\frac{1}{3}}T^{\frac{2}{3}}\Big).

Taking this together with (
97
) and (
100
) establishes the claimed regret bound for the smooth drift setting.

C.3
Proof of auxiliary lemmas

C.3.1
Proof of Lemma
C.1

For notational convenience, we write
Z_{1:m}
here in place of
Z_{1:m}^{\mathsf{cal}}
as long as it is clear from the context.

To apply McDiarmid’s inequality, consider two given calibration datasets,

z_{1:m}
and
z_{1:m}^{\prime}
, which differ in exactly one sample.
For any given point
(x,y)\in{\mathcal{Z}}
, define the corresponding scores as

s^{(X,Y)}_{z_{1:m}}(x,y)\coloneqq\bigl|y-\widehat{\mu}^{(X,Y)}_{z_{1:m}}(x)\bigr|,\qquad s^{(X,Y)}_{z^{\prime}_{1:m}}(x,y)\coloneqq\bigl|y-\widehat{\mu}^{(X,Y)}_{z^{\prime}_{1:m}}(x)\bigr|.

Note that both
\widehat{\mu}^{(X,Y)}_{z_{1:m}}(\cdot)
and
\widehat{\mu}^{(X,Y)}_{z_{1:m}^{\prime}}(\cdot)
are trained on
n+1
data points. Then, by Assumption
4.3
, for any
x\in{\mathcal{X}}
we have

\left|{\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(x)-\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:m}^{\prime}}^{(X,Y)}(x)}\right|\leq\frac{L_{2}}{n}.

Combining this with Assumption
4.2
, which assumes the Lipschitz continuity of the distribution function, we see that: for
(X,Y)\sim\mathcal{D}
,

\displaystyle\left|{{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\left(s_{z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)>u\right)-{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\left(s_{z_{1:m}^{\prime}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)>u\right)}\right|

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{\mathrm{(a)}}}{\leq}{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\left(u-\Delta\leq s_{z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)\leq u+\Delta\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(b)}}{\leq}4L_{1}\sup\limits_{X,Y}\left\{\left|{\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X)-\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:m}^{\prime}}^{(X,Y)}(X)}\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4L_{1}L_{2}}{n}=\frac{4L}{n},

(116)

where
\Delta\coloneqq\big|\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X)-\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:m}^{\prime}}^{(X,Y)}(X)\big|
. Here, (a)
is a result of Fact
C.1

whereas (b) follows since

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\left(u-\Delta\leq s_{z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)\leq u+\Delta\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\left(-u-\Delta\leq Y-\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}\leq-u+\Delta\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\left(u-\Delta\leq Y-\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}\leq u+\Delta\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sup\limits_{\mu\in{\mathbb{R}}}{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\Bigl((\mu-u)-\Delta^{\prime}\leq Y\leq\Delta^{\prime}+(\mu-u)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sup\limits_{\mu\in{\mathbb{R}}}{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\Bigl((\mu+u)-\Delta^{\prime}\leq Y\leq\Delta^{\prime}+(\mu+u)\Bigr)\leq 4L_{1}\Delta^{\prime}

(117)

with
\Delta^{\prime}\coloneqq\sup\limits_{X,Y}\left\{\big|\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X)-\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:m}^{\prime}}^{(X,Y)}(X)\big|\right\}
.

From (
116
), we observe that
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathcal{D}}\big(s_{z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)>u\big)
—when viewed as a function of
z_{1:m}
—satisfies the bounded difference property with coefficient
4L/n
.
Now, we make the following definition:

\displaystyle Q(z_{1:m},u)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left|{{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\left(s_{z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)>u\right)-{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}\times{\mathcal{D}}}\left(s_{Z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)>u\right)}\right|,

\displaystyle Q(z_{1:m})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\big\{Q(z_{1:m},u)\big\},

where

{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}\times{\mathcal{D}}}\left(s_{Z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)>u\right)\coloneqq{\mathbb{E}}_{Z_{1:m}\sim\mathcal{D}_{1:m}}\left[{\mathbb{P}}_{(X,Y)\sim{\mathcal{D}}}\left(s_{Z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)>u\,\Big|\,Z_{1:m}\right)\right].

(118)

Then, basic calculation yields

\displaystyle Q(z_{1:m})-Q(z_{1:m}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\big\{Q(z_{1:m},u)\big\}-\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\big\{Q(z_{1:m}^{\prime},u)\big\}

\displaystyle\leq\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\big\{Q(z_{1:m},u)-Q(z_{1:m}^{\prime},u)\big\}\leq\frac{4L}{n}.

Hence, by applying McDiarmid’s inequality (Lemma
E.1
), we can demonstrate that

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}

\displaystyle\left\{\left|{{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\left(s_{Z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)>u~\Big|~Z_{1:m}\right)-{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}\times{\mathcal{D}}}\left(s_{Z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)>u\right)}\right|\right\}=Q(Z_{1:m})

(119)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{Z_{1:m}}\left[Q(Z_{1:m})\right]+4{L}\frac{\sqrt{m\log\frac{1}{\delta}}}{n}

holds with probability exceeding
1-{\delta}
.

Now consider any given
z_{0}=(x_{0},y_{0})
, and denote
\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:m}}^{0}(\cdot)\coloneqq\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:m}}^{(x_{0},y_{0})}(\cdot)
and
s_{z_{1:m}}^{0}(x,y)=\big|y-\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:m}}^{0}(x)\big|
.
Let
Z_{1:m}^{*}
be an independent copy of
Z_{1:m}
.
Then one can show that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{Z_{1:m}}[Q(Z_{1:m})]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{Z_{1:m}}\left[\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left|{{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\big(s_{Z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)>u\mid Z_{1:m}\big)-{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}\times{\mathcal{D}}}\big(s_{Z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)>u\big)}\right|\right]

(120)

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Z_{1:m},Z_{1:m}^{*}}\left[\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left|{{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\big(s_{Z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)>u\mid Z_{1:m}\big)-{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\big(s_{Z_{1:m}^{*}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)>u\mid Z_{1:m}^{*}\big)}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq 4L_{1}\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Z_{1:m},Z_{1:m}^{*}}\left[\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left\{{\mathbb{E}}_{X}\left[\left|{\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}}^{0}(X)-\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}^{*}}^{0}(X)}\right|+\frac{L_{2}}{n}\right]\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=4L_{1}\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Z_{1:m},Z_{1:m}^{*},X}\left[\left|{\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}}^{0}(X)-\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}^{*}}^{0}(X)}\right|\right]+\frac{8L}{n}.

Here, the second line follows from Jensen’s inequality; the penultimate line follows since, for any given two arrays
z_{1:m}
and
z_{1:m}^{*}
, one has

\displaystyle\bigg|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\Big(s_{z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)>u\Big)-{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\Big(s_{z_{1:m}^{*}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)>u\Big)\,\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\Big(s_{z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)>u\Big)-{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\Big(s_{z_{1:m}}^{0}(X,Y)>u\Big)\,\bigg|{+}\bigg|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\Big(s_{z_{1:m}^{*}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)>u\Big)-{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\Big(s_{z_{1:m}^{*}}^{0}(X,Y)>u\Big)\,\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\Big(s_{z_{1:m}}^{0}(X,Y)>u\Big)-{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\Big(s_{z_{1:m}^{*}}^{0}(X,Y)>u\Big)\,\bigg|

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(c)}}{\leq}\frac{8L}{n}+\bigg|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\Big(s_{z_{1:m}}^{0}(X,Y)>u\Big)-{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\Big(s_{z_{1:m}^{*}}^{0}(X,Y)>u\Big)\,\bigg|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Fact}~\ref{fact:ind_diff}}{\leq}\frac{8L}{n}+{\mathbb{E}}_{X}\left[{\mathbb{P}}\left(u-\big|\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:m}}^{0}(X)-\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:m}*}^{0}(X)\big|\leq s_{z_{1:m}}^{0}(X,Y)\leq u+\big|\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:m}}^{0}(X)-\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:m}*}^{0}(X)\big|\,\Big|\,X\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(d)}}{\leq}\frac{8L}{n}+4L_{1}{\mathbb{E}}_{X}\left[\left|{\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:m}}^{0}(X)-\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:m}^{*}}^{0}(X)}\right|\right],

where (c) holds by Fact
C.1
,
Assumptions
4.2
and
4.3
(similar to the arguments for (
116
)), and (d) makes use of Assumptions
4.2
(similar to the arguments for (
117
)).

To control the first term on the right-hand side of (
120
), we introduce the quantity below for any given
X
:

\nu_{i}(X)\coloneqq{\mathbb{E}}\left[\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}}^{0}(X)\,\big|\,Z_{1:i+1}\right]-{\mathbb{E}}\left[\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}}^{0}(X)\,\big|\,Z_{1:i}\right],\quad i=0,\ldots,m-1.

It is readily seen that
\{\nu_{i}(X)\}_{i=0}^{m-1}
forms a martingale difference sequence.
Moreover, Assumption
4.2
tells us that, for any
i=0,\ldots,m-1
and
X\in{\mathcal{X}}
,

\left|{\nu_{i}(X)}\right|\leq\sup\limits_{z_{1:i+1}}{\mathbb{E}}_{Z_{i+2:m}}\left[\left|{\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:i}\cup z_{i+1}\cup Z_{i+2:m}}^{0}(X)-{\mathbb{E}}_{Z_{i+1}}\left[\widehat{\mu}_{z_{1:i}\cup Z_{i+1}\cup Z_{i+2:m}}^{0}(X)\right]}\right|\right]\leq\frac{L_{2}}{n}.

Therefore, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Z_{1:m},Z_{1:m}^{*},X}\left[\left|{\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}}^{0}(X)-\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}^{*}}^{0}(X)}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}}\left[\left|{\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}}^{0}(X)-{\mathbb{E}}_{\widetilde{Z}_{1:m}\sim{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}}\big[\widehat{\mu}_{\widetilde{Z}_{1:m}}^{0}(X)\big]}\right|\right]

(121)

\displaystyle\leq 2{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}}\left[\left|{\sum\limits_{{i}={0}}^{m-1}\nu_{i}(X)}\right|\right]\leq 2\left({\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}}\left[\left(\sum\limits_{{i}={0}}^{m-1}\nu_{i}(X)\right)^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=2\left(\sum\limits_{{i}={0}}^{m-1}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}}\left[\nu_{i}(X)^{2}\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{2L_{2}\sqrt{m}}{n},

where the last equality holds since
\{\nu_{i}(X)\}
is a martingale difference sequence.
Taking (
119
), (
120
) and (
121
) together yields that, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}

\displaystyle\left\{\left|{{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}}\left(s_{Z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)>u~\Big|~Z_{1:m}\right)-{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}\times{\mathcal{D}}}\left(s_{Z_{1:m}}^{(X,Y)}(X,Y)>u\right)}\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 4L\frac{\sqrt{m\log(1/\delta)}}{n}+\frac{8L}{n}+8L\frac{\sqrt{m\log(1/\delta)}}{n}\leq 16L\frac{\sqrt{m\log(1/\delta)}}{n},

thereby concluding the proof of Lemma
C.1
.

C.3.2
Proof of Lemma
C.2

Before proceeding, we introduce several additional convenient notations below.

•

Z_{1:m}
: we often use it in place of
Z_{1:m}^{\mathsf{cal}}
when there is no ambiguity.

•

\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}}(\cdot)
: we remind the reader that this indicates the fitted model trained on
Z_{1:m}\cup z_{m+1:n}^{\mathsf{train}}
(see Definition
C.1
).

•

Z_{1:m}^{x}
: this refers to
\{(x_{i},Y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{m}
, where the features are frozen to be
\{x_{1},\dots,x_{m}\}
; with this notation one clearly has
Z_{1:m}^{X}=Z_{1:m}
.

•

\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}^{x}}(\cdot)
: the fitted model trained on
\{(x_{i},Y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{m}
, with fixed features
\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}
and random responses
\{Y_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}
.

•

\widetilde{\mu}_{x_{1:m}}(x_{i})
: the expected prediction of
\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}^{x}}(\cdot)
w.r.t.
x_{i}
, taken over the randomness of
Y_{1:m}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}_{x_{1:m}}(x_{i})\coloneqq{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:m}\mid X_{1:m}}\left[\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}^{x}}(x_{i})\mid X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}\right],\qquad\text{ }i=1,\ldots,m,

(122)

The proof of this lemma is organized into several steps below.

Step 1: proximity of
\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}^{x}}
and its conditional expectation.

Equipped with the above set of notation, we immediately note that:

•

\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}}(\cdot)
is a function jointly dependent on the random objects
X_{1:m}
and
Y_{1:m}
;

•

For any fixed realization
x_{1:m}
, the collection

\{\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}^{x}}(x_{i})\}_{i=1}^{m}

can be viewed as a family of functions dependent on the random variables
Y_{1:m}
;

•

Conditional on
X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}
,
the random variables
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m}
are mutually independent.

We now make note of the following basic fact.

Claim C.2
.

Recall the definition of
\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}^{x}}(x_{i})
and
\widetilde{\mu}_{x_{1:m}}(x_{i})
defined at the beginning of this subsection. Then
for any fixed
x_{1:m}
and any
0<\delta<1
, the event

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{1}(x_{1:m})\coloneqq\left\{\sup\limits_{i\in\{1,\dots,m\}}\left\{\left|{\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}^{x}}(x_{i})-\widetilde{\mu}_{x_{1:m}}(x_{i})}\right|\right\}\leq\frac{L_{2}}{n}\sqrt{m\log\frac{10m}{\delta}}\right\}

(123)

occurs with probability at least
1-\delta/5
.

Proof.

This claim follows directly by invoking McDiarmid’s inequality (Lemma
E.1
) along with Assumption
4.3
and a union bound over
i\in\{1,\dots,m\}
. We omit the details for brevity.
∎

Step 2: a surrogate empirical distribution.

With Claim
C.2
in place, our next step is to approximate the target empirical distribution

\widehat{F}_{Z_{1:m}}(u)\coloneqq\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\mathbbm{1}\left\{s_{i}\leq u\right\}

(124)

using a surrogate empirical distribution

\widetilde{F}_{Z_{1:m}}(u)\coloneqq\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\right\},\quad\text{ where }\widetilde{s}_{i}\coloneqq\left|{Y_{i}-\widetilde{\mu}_{X_{1:m}}(X_{i})}\right|,~~i=1,\ldots,m.

(125)

In words, the fitted outcome
\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}}(X_{i})
is now replaced with
\widetilde{\mu}_{X_{1:m}}(X_{i})
, the latter of which averages out the randomness over
Y_{1:m}
(cf. (
122
)).
On the event
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}(X_{1:m})
defined in (
123
), we can bound the difference between these two quantities as follows

\displaystyle\Big|

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{Z_{1:m}}(u)-\widehat{F}_{Z_{1:m}}(u)\Big|\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\big|\mathbbm{1}\{s_{i}>u\}-\mathbbm{1}\{\widetilde{s}_{i}>u\}\big|

(126)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(a)}}{\leq}\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\mathbbm{1}\big\{u-\left|{s_{i}-\widetilde{s}_{i}}\right|<\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u+\left|{s_{i}-\widetilde{s}_{i}}\right|\big\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\mathbbm{1}\big\{u-\left|{\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}}(X_{i}){-}\widetilde{\mu}_{X_{1:m}}(X_{i})}\right|<\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u+\left|{\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}}(X_{i}){-}\widetilde{\mu}_{X_{1:m}}(X_{i})}\right|\big\}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(b)}}{\leq}\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\mathbbm{1}\left\{u-\frac{L_{2}}{n}\sqrt{{m}\log\frac{10{m}}{\delta}}<\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u+\frac{L_{2}}{n}\sqrt{{m}\log\frac{10{m}}{\delta}}\right\},

where (a) holds because of Fact
C.1

and (b) arises from the definition of the event
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}(X_{1:m})
.

For simplicity of notation, denote

\displaystyle\Delta_{n,m}\coloneqq\frac{L_{2}}{n}\sqrt{{m}\log\frac{10{m}}{\delta}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\mathcal{B}}(u,\varepsilon)\coloneqq(u-\varepsilon,u+\varepsilon],

(127)

and consider the event

{\mathcal{E}}_{2}(x_{1:m})\coloneqq\left\{\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left\{\frac{1}{m}\left|{\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\Big(\mathbbm{1}\{\widetilde{s}_{i}\in{\mathcal{B}}(u,\Delta_{n,m})\}{-}{\mathbb{P}}\big(\widetilde{s}_{i}\in{\mathcal{B}}(u,\Delta_{n,m})\,|\,X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}\big)\Big)}\right|\right\}\leq 10\sqrt{\frac{\log(10/\delta)}{m}}\right\}.

We would like to prove that this event occurs with high probability conditional on
X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}
.
Towards this end, we first observe that, for any
i=1,\ldots,m
and any
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}\left\{\widetilde{s}_{i}\in{\mathcal{B}}(u,\Delta_{n,m})\right\}

\displaystyle-{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(\widetilde{s}_{i}\in{\mathcal{B}}(u,\Delta_{n,m})\,|\,X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\Bigl(\mathbbm{1}\{\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u+\Delta_{n,m}\}-{\mathbb{P}}\big(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u+\Delta_{n,m}\,|\,X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}\big)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\quad-\Bigl(\mathbbm{1}\{\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u-\Delta_{n,m}\}-{\mathbb{P}}\big(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u-\Delta_{n,m}\,|\,X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}\big)\Bigr),

which in turn implies that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\biggl|\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\Bigl(\mathbbm{1}\{\widetilde{s}_{i}\in{\mathcal{B}}(u,\Delta_{n,m})\}

\displaystyle-{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(\widetilde{s}_{i}\in{\mathcal{B}}(u,\Delta_{n,m})\,|\,X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}\bigr)\Bigr)\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\biggl|\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\Bigl(\mathbbm{1}\{\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u+\Delta_{n,m})\}-{\mathbb{P}}\big(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u+\Delta_{n,m}\,|\,X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}\big)\Bigr)\biggr|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{m}\biggl|\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\Bigl(\mathbbm{1}\{\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u-\Delta_{n,m})\}-{\mathbb{P}}\big(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u-\Delta_{n,m}\,|\,X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}\big)\Bigr)\biggr|.

(128)

In addition, it is straightforward to verify that: conditional on
X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}
,
the quantity
\widetilde{s}_{i}
(see (
125
) and (
122
)) is independent of
Y_{1:m}\setminus\{Y_{i}\}
, so that
the collection
\{\widetilde{s}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}

forms a set of mutually independent random variables.
Hence,
Lemma

E.4
readily tells us that, conditional on
X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}
,

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left\{\frac{1}{m}\left|{\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\Bigl(\mathbbm{1}\{\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u+\Delta_{m,n}\}-{\mathbb{P}}\big(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u+\Delta_{n,m}\,|\,X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}\big)\Bigr)}\right|\right\}\leq 5\sqrt{\frac{\log(10/\delta)}{m}}

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left\{\frac{1}{m}\left|{\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\Bigl(\mathbbm{1}\{\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u-\Delta_{m,n}\}-{\mathbb{P}}\big(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u-\Delta_{n,m}\,|\,X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}\big)\Bigr)}\right|\right\}\leq 5\sqrt{\frac{\log(10/\delta)}{m}}

hold with probability exceeding
1-\delta/5
, which taken together with (
128
) shows that for any realization
x_{1:m}
,

{\mathbb{P}}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{2}(x_{1:m})\mid X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}\right)\geq 1-\frac{\delta}{5}.

(129)

Continuing from the derivation in Eqn. (
126
), we can now see that: on the event
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}(x_{1:m})\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{2}(x_{1:m})
,
for any
u\in{\mathbb{R}}
we have

\displaystyle\left|{\widetilde{F}_{Z_{1:m}}(u)-\widehat{F}_{Z_{1:m}}(u)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\widetilde{s}_{i}\in{\mathcal{B}}(u,\Delta_{n,m})\right\}

(130)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}{\mathbb{P}}\big(\widetilde{s}_{i}\in{\mathcal{B}}(u,\Delta_{n,m})\,|\,X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}\big)+10\sqrt{\frac{\log({10}/{\delta})}{m}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}2L_{1}\Delta_{n,m}+10\sqrt{\frac{\log(10/\delta)}{m}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2{L}}{n}\sqrt{{m}\log\frac{10{m}}{\delta}}+10\sqrt{\frac{\log(10/\delta)}{m}},

where the second line is valid on
{\mathcal{E}}_{2}(x_{1:m})
, and the third line makes use of the assumption stated in Assumption
4.2
,
along with the fact that the interval
{\mathcal{B}}(u,\varepsilon)
has length
2\varepsilon
.
As a remark, in view of Claim
C.2
and (
129
), we know that (
130
) holds with high probability when conditioned on
X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}
.

Step 3: the surrogate empirical distribution vs. the marginal coverage rate.

Next, we would like to bound the discrepancy between the surrogate empirical distribution
\widetilde{F}_{Z_{1:m}}(u)
(cf. (
125
)) and the marginal coverage rate

\displaystyle\overline{F}_{m}(u)\coloneqq\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}}\big(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\big).

(131)

Towards this end, we find it convenient to introduce two auxiliary coverage rates conditional on
X_{1:m}
as intermediary quantities, namely,

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{X_{1:m}}(u)\coloneqq\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid X_{i}}\left(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u~\big|~X_{1:m}\right),\quad\overline{F}_{X_{1:m}}(u)\coloneqq\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid X_{i}}(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\mid X_{i}),

(132)

where for any
i=1,\ldots,m
, we define

{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid X_{i}}(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\mid X_{i})\coloneqq{\mathbb{E}}_{X_{1:m}\backslash\{X_{i}\}}\big[{\mathbb{P}}(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\mid X_{1:m})\big].

It then follows from the triangle inequality that

\displaystyle\left|{\widetilde{F}_{Z_{1:m}}(u)-\overline{F}_{m}(u)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|{\widetilde{F}_{Z_{1:m}}(u)-\widetilde{F}_{X_{1:m}}(u)}\right|+\left|{\widetilde{F}_{X_{1:m}}(u)-\overline{F}_{X_{1:m}}(u)}\right|+\left|{\overline{F}_{X_{1:m}}(u)-\overline{F}_{m}(u)}\right|

(133)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{m}\biggl|\,\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\Bigl(\mathbbm{1}\big\{\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\big\}-{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid X_{1:m}}\left(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u~\big|~X_{1:m}\right)\Bigr)\,\biggr|}_{\eqqcolon{\mathcal{T}}_{1}(u,Z_{1:m})}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{1}{m}\biggl|\,\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\Bigl({\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid X_{1:m}}\left(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\,\big|\,X_{1:m}\right)-{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid X_{i}}(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\mid X_{i})\Bigr)\,\biggr|}_{\eqqcolon{\mathcal{T}}_{2}(u,X_{1:m})}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{1}{m}\biggl|\,\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\left({\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid X_{i}}\left(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\,\big|\,X_{i}\right)-{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}}\left(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\right)\right)\,\biggr|}_{\eqqcolon{\mathcal{T}}_{3}(u,X_{1:m})},

thus leaving us with three terms to cope with.

Step 4: a bound on the first term in (
133
).

Regarding the first term
{\mathcal{T}}_{1}(u,Z_{1:m})
on the right-hand side of (
133
), consider the following event w.r.t. a given realization
x_{1:m}
:

{\mathcal{E}}_{3}(x_{1:m})\coloneqq\left\{\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}{\mathcal{T}}_{1}(u,Z_{1:m})\leq 5\sqrt{\frac{\log(5/\delta)}{m}}\right\}.

(134)

As mentioned earlier, when conditioned on
X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}
,
the random variables
\{\widetilde{s}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{m}
are mutually independent, which allows us to invoke the generalized DKW inequality (
Lemma

E.4
) to show that

{\mathbb{P}}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{3}(x_{1:m})\mid X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}\right)\geq 1-\delta/5.

(135)

Step 5: a bound on the second term in (
133
).

Regarding the second term
{\mathcal{T}}_{2}(u,X_{1:m})
on the right-hand side of (
133
), we first single out a few properties about
\overline{F}_{X_{1:m}}(u)
(cf. (
132
)).
Without loss of generality, consider two realizations
x_{1:m}
and
x_{1:m}^{\prime}
that differ only in the first sample (i.e.,
x_{1}\neq x_{1}^{\prime}
). We would like to show that, for any
i\geq 1
, the function

{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid x_{1:m}}\big(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\mid X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}\big)
,
viewed as a function of
x_{1:m}
, satisfies the bounded difference property. In fact, in view of the definition of
\widetilde{\mu}_{x_{1:m}}(x_{i})
(cf. (
122
)), we have

\displaystyle\left|{\widetilde{\mu}_{x_{1:m}}(x_{i})-\widetilde{\mu}_{x_{1:m}^{\prime}}(x_{i})}\right|=\left|{{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:m}\mid x_{1:m}}\left[\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}^{x}}(x_{i})\right]-{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:m}^{\prime}\mid x_{1:m}^{\prime}}\left[\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1,m}^{x^{\prime}}}(x_{i})\right]}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\overset{\mathrm{(a)}}{\leq}\left|{{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{2:m}\mid x_{2:m}}\Big[{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1}\mid x_{1}}[\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}^{x}}(x_{i})\mid Y_{2:m}]-{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1}^{\prime}\mid x_{1}^{\prime}}[\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}^{x^{\prime}}}(x_{i})\mid Y_{2:m}]\Big]}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{2:m}\mid x_{2:m}}\Big[{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1}\times Y_{1}^{\prime}|(x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime})}\left[\left|{\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1}^{x}\cup Z_{2:m}^{x}}(x_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1}^{x^{\prime}}\cup Z_{2:m}^{x}}(x_{i})}\right|~\big|~Y_{2:m}\right]\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\overset{\mathrm{(b)}}{\leq}{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{2:m}\mid x_{2:m}}\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1}\times Y_{1}^{\prime}\mid(x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime})}\left[\frac{L_{2}}{n}~\Big|~Y_{2:m}\right]\right]=\frac{L_{2}}{n},

(136)

where we denote
Z_{1:m}^{x^{\prime}}\coloneqq\{(x_{i}^{\prime},Y_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i=1}^{m}
,
Z_{1}^{x}\coloneqq(x_{1},Y_{1})
, and
Z_{1}^{x^{\prime}}\coloneqq(x_{1}^{\prime},Y_{1}^{\prime})
.
Here, (b) results from Assumption
4.3
. Regarding (a), it follows from the fact that
x_{2:m}=x^{\prime}_{2:m}
;
in particular,
Y_{2:m}
and
Y^{\prime}_{2:m}
have the same joint distribution, and are both independent of
(Y_{1},Y^{\prime}_{1})
, which allow us to couple
Y_{1:m}
and
Y^{\prime}_{1:m}
, so that the two samples differ only at the first data point, i.e.,
(x_{1},Y_{1})\neq(x^{\prime}_{1},Y^{\prime}_{1})
.
Consequently, combining (
136
) with Assumption
4.2
reveals that, for any
i=2,\ldots,m
,

\displaystyle\Big|~{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid x_{i}}\left(\left|{Y_{i}-\widetilde{\mu}_{x_{1:m}}(x_{i})}\right|>u\right)-{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid x_{i}}\left(\left|{Y_{i}-\widetilde{\mu}_{x_{1:m}^{\prime}}(x_{i})}\right|>u\right)\,\Big|

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\Big(u-\left|{\widetilde{\mu}_{x_{1:m}}(x_{i})-\widetilde{\mu}_{x_{1:m}^{\prime}}(x_{i})}\right|\leq\left|{Y_{i}-\widetilde{\mu}_{x_{1:m}}(x_{i})}\right|\leq u+\left|{\widetilde{\mu}_{x_{1:m}}(x_{i})-\widetilde{\mu}_{x_{1:m}^{\prime}}(x_{i})}\right|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 2L_{1}\left|{\widetilde{\mu}_{x_{1:m}}(x_{i})-\widetilde{\mu}_{x_{1:m}^{\prime}}(x_{i})}\right|\leq\frac{2L_{1}L_{2}}{n}=\frac{2{L}}{n}.

(137)

Now, let us return to
\overline{F}_{x_{1:m}}(u)
. Applying the above bound (
137
) along with straightforward calculations reveals that, for any
x
,

\displaystyle\left|{\widetilde{F}_{x_{1:m}}(u)-\widetilde{F}_{x_{1:m}^{\prime}}(u)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\left|{{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{1}\mid x_{1}}\big(\widetilde{s}_{1}\leq u\mid x_{1:m}\big)-{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{1}^{\prime}\mid x_{1}^{\prime}}\big(\widetilde{s}_{1}^{\prime}\leq u\mid x_{1:m}^{\prime}\big)}\right|

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m}\left|{\sum\limits_{{i}={2}}^{m}\Big({\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid x_{i}}\big(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\mid x_{1:m}^{\prime}\big)-{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid x_{i}}\big(\widetilde{s}_{i}^{\prime}\leq u\mid x_{1:m}^{\prime}\big)\Big)}\right|\leq\frac{1}{m}+\frac{2{L}}{n},

where
\widetilde{s}_{i}^{\prime}\coloneqq\bigl|Y_{i}^{\prime}-\widetilde{\mu}_{x_{1:m}^{\prime}}(x_{i}^{\prime})\bigr|
.
Also, note that
{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid x_{i}}(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\mid x_{i})
is a function of
x_{i}
only, and hence

\bigl|\,\overline{F}_{x_{1:m}}(u)-\overline{F}_{x_{1:m}^{\prime}}(u)\,\bigr|=\frac{1}{m}\bigl|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{1}\mid x_{1}}(\widetilde{s}_{1}\leq u\mid x_{1})-{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{1}\mid x_{1}^{\prime}}(\widetilde{s}_{1}^{\prime}\leq u\mid x_{1}^{\prime})\,\bigr|\leq\frac{1}{m}.

As a consequence, for any
x
, the function
\bigl|\,\widetilde{F}_{x_{1:m}}(u)-\overline{F}_{x_{1:m}}(u)\,\bigr|
satisfies the bounded difference property
with coefficient
\tfrac{2}{m}+\tfrac{2{L}}{n}
.
If we define

{\mathcal{T}}_{2}(x_{1:m})\coloneqq\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left\{{\mathcal{T}}_{2}(u,x_{1:m})\right\},

then simple computation yields

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}_{2}(x_{1:m})-{\mathcal{T}}_{2}(x_{1:m}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left\{{\mathcal{T}}_{2}(u,x_{1:m})\right\}-\sup\limits_{u^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left\{{\mathcal{T}}_{2}(u^{\prime},x_{1:m}^{\prime})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left\{{\mathcal{T}}_{2}(u,x_{1:m})-{\mathcal{T}}_{2}(u,x_{1:m}^{\prime})\right\}\leq\frac{2}{m}+\frac{2L}{n}.

Thus, we can apply McDiarmid’s inequality (Lemma
E.1
) to derive

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left\{\left|{\widetilde{F}_{X_{1:m}}(u)-\overline{F}_{X_{1:m}}(u)}\right|\right\}

\displaystyle={\mathcal{T}}_{2}(X_{1:m})

(138)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{X_{1:m}}\left[{\mathcal{T}}_{2}(X_{1:m})\right]+\left(\frac{2}{m}+\frac{2L}{n}\right)\sqrt{{m}\log\frac{5}{\delta}}

holds with probability at least
1-\delta/5
.

It then comes down to bounding
{\mathbb{E}}[{\mathcal{T}}_{2}(X_{1:m})]
. From the definition of
{\mathcal{T}}_{2}(X_{1:m})
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[{\mathcal{T}}_{2}(X_{1:m})]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{X_{1:m}}\left[\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left|{\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\Bigl(\,{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid X_{i}}(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\mid X_{1:m})-{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid X_{i}}(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\mid X_{i})\,\Bigr)}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{X_{1:m}}\left[\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\biggl|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid X_{i}}(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\mid X_{1:m})-{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid X_{i}}(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\mid X_{i})\,\biggr|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}{\mathbb{E}}_{X_{1:m}}\left[\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\biggl|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid X_{i}}(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\mid X_{1:m})-{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid X_{i}}(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\mid X_{i})\,\biggr|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}{\mathbb{E}}_{X_{i}}\Biggl[\,\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{X_{1:m}\backslash\{X_{i}\}}\biggl[\,\underbrace{\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\biggl|\,{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid X_{i}}(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\mid X_{1:m})-{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid X_{i}}(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\mid X_{i})\,\biggr|}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(X_{1:m})}\,\Big|\,X_{i}\,\biggr]\,\Biggr],

(139)

which motivates us to control
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(X_{1:m}),~i=1,\ldots,m
. Without loss of generality, it suffices to analyze
{\mathcal{K}}_{1}(X_{1:m})
, since the same argument applies to the remaining
i
. Fix
X_{1}=x_{1}
, it is seen that
\widetilde{\mu}_{X_{1:m}}(\cdot)
satisfies Assumption
4.3
with parameter
L_{2}/n
with respect to the remaining samples
X_{2:m}
. Applying
Lemma

C.1
reveals that, for any
x_{1}
(it can be regarded as the target sample is
(X_{1},Y_{1})
and
X_{1}\sim\delta_{\{x_{1}\}}
at this time),

{\mathcal{K}}_{1}(\{x_{1}\}\cup X_{2:m})\leq\frac{16L}{n}\sqrt{m\log n}

holds with probability at least
1-1/n
, which combined with (
139
) gives

{\mathbb{E}}[{\mathcal{T}}_{2}(X_{1:m})]\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}{\mathbb{E}}_{X_{i}}\left[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{X_{1:m}\backslash\{X_{i}\}}\left[{\mathcal{K}}_{i}(X_{1:m})\mid X_{i}\right]\right]\leq\frac{16L}{n}\sqrt{m\log n}+\frac{1}{n}.

Plugging this into (
138
) yields that the following event

{\mathcal{E}}_{4}\coloneqq\left\{\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\{{\mathcal{T}}_{2}(u,X_{1:m})\}\leq 3\sqrt{\frac{\log({5}/{\delta})}{m}}+\frac{18L}{n}\sqrt{{m}\log\left(\frac{5}{\delta}+n\right)}\right\}

(140)

happens with probability at least
1-\delta/5
.

Step 6: a bound on the last term in (
133
).

We now turn attention to the last term
{\mathcal{T}}_{3}(u,X_{1:m})
on the right-hand side of (
133
).
Define

H(u,X_{i})\coloneqq{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{i}\mid X_{i}}(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u\mid X_{i}),\qquad i=1,\ldots,m.

It is easily seen that the random variables
\{H(u,X_{i})\}_{1\leq i\leq m}

are mutually independent. Moreover, for any
i
and any fixed
X_{i}
,
H(u,X_{i})
is a non-decreasing function in
u
.
Applying
Lemma

E.4
reveals that the event

{\mathcal{E}}_{5}\coloneqq\left\{\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left\{\big|\overline{F}_{X_{1:m}}(u)-\overline{F}_{m}(u)\big|\right\}\leq 5\sqrt{\frac{\log(5/\delta)}{m}}\right\}

(141)

happens with probability at least
1-\delta/5
, where we remind the reader of the definitions of
\overline{F}_{X_{1:m}}
and
\overline{F}_{m}
in (
132
) and (
131
), respectively.

Step 7: putting all pieces together.

To finish up, let us put together the preceding results.
First, define

F_{m}(u)\coloneqq\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}}\bigl(s_{i}\leq u\bigr).

(142)

On the event
\left(\bigcap_{i=1}^{3}{\mathcal{E}}_{i}(x_{1:m})\right)\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{4}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{5}
, we see that for any
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
, it always holds that

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}

\displaystyle\left|{\widehat{F}_{Z_{1:m}}(u)-{F}_{m}(u)}\right|\leq\left|{\widehat{F}_{Z_{1:m}}(u)-\widetilde{F}_{Z_{1:m}}(u)}\right|+\left|{\widetilde{F}_{Z_{1:m}}(u)-F_{m}(u)}\right|

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:F_X-F_m_decomp}}{\leq}\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\Bigl\{\left|{\widehat{F}_{Z_{1:m}}(u)-\widetilde{F}_{Z_{1:m}}(u)}\right|\Bigr\}+\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left\{{\mathcal{T}}_{1}(u,Z_{1:m})\right\}

\displaystyle\quad+\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left\{{\mathcal{T}}_{2}(u,Z_{1:m})\right\}+\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left\{{\mathcal{T}}_{3}(u,Z_{1:m})\right\}+\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left\{\left|{\overline{F}_{m}(u)-F_{m}(u)}\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2{L}}{n}\sqrt{{m}\log\frac{10{m}}{\delta}}+10\sqrt{\frac{\log({10}/{\delta})}{m}}+5\sqrt{\frac{\log(5/\delta)}{m}}

\displaystyle\quad+3\sqrt{\frac{\log(5/\delta)}{m}}+\frac{18L}{n}\sqrt{{m}\log\left(\frac{5}{\delta}+n\right)}+5\sqrt{\frac{\log(5/\delta)}{m}}+\sup\limits_{u\in{\mathbb{R}}}\left\{\left|{\overline{F}_{m}(u)-F_{m}(u)}\right|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq 24\sqrt{\frac{\log(10/\delta)}{m}}+\frac{24L}{n}\sqrt{{m}\log\left(\frac{10{m}}{\delta}+n\right)}.

To justify the last inequality, we observe that, for any
u
,

\displaystyle\left|{\overline{F}_{m}(u)-F_{m}(u)}\right|\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\left|{{\mathbb{P}}(\widetilde{s}_{i}\leq u)-{\mathbb{P}}({s}_{i}\leq u)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}\Bigg\{{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\widetilde{s}_{i}-\left|{\widetilde{\mu}_{X_{1:m}}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}}(X_{i})}\right|\leq u\Bigr)-{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\widetilde{s}_{i}+\left|{\widetilde{\mu}_{X_{1:m}}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}}(X_{i})}\right|\leq u\Bigr)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(a)}}{\leq}\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\widetilde{s}_{i}\in{\mathcal{B}}\left(u,\Delta\right)\right)+\frac{1}{m}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m}{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(\left|{\widetilde{\mu}_{X_{1:m}}(X_{i})-\widehat{\mu}_{Z_{1:m}}(X_{i})}\right|>\Delta\bigr)\leq\frac{4{L}}{n}\sqrt{{m}\log n}+\frac{1}{n},

where
\Delta\coloneqq\frac{2L_{2}}{n}\sqrt{{m}\log n}
, and (a) follows by invoking the same argument as in the analysis of
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}(X_{1:m})
.
By combining our uniform high-probability bounds on
{\mathcal{E}}_{i}(x_{1:m})
for
i=1,2,3
given any
X_{1:m}=x_{1:m}
, and applying the high-probability bound of
{\mathcal{E}}_{4}
and
{\mathcal{E}}_{5}
as well as the union bound, we arrive at

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}}

\displaystyle\left(\left\{\left(\bigcap\limits_{i=1}^{3}{\mathcal{E}}_{i}(X_{1:m})\right)\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{4}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{5}\right\}^{\mathrm{c}}\right)={\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}}\left(\left(\bigcup{\mathcal{E}}_{i}(X_{1:m})^{\mathrm{c}}\right)\cup{\mathcal{E}}_{4}^{\mathrm{c}}\cup{\mathcal{E}}_{5}^{\mathrm{c}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{3}{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{1:m}}\big({\mathcal{E}}_{i}(X_{1:m})^{\mathrm{c}}\big)+\frac{\delta}{5}+\frac{\delta}{5}=\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{3}{\mathbb{E}}_{X_{1:m}}\left[{\mathbb{P}}_{Y_{1:m}\mid X_{1:m}}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{i}(X_{1:m})^{\mathrm{c}}\mid X_{1:m}\right)\right]+\frac{2\delta}{5}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\delta}{5}+\frac{2\delta}{5}=\delta.

This completes the proof of
Lemma

C.2
.

C.3.3
Proof of
Lemma

C.3

The proof closely follows the arguments in the proof of
Lemmas

B.1
and
B.2
. The only difference lies in that, for the smooth drift setting, inequality (
71
) in the pretrained-score setting needs to be modified as follows:

\displaystyle A_{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left|{{\mathcal{I}}_{k}}\right|}\sum_{l\in{\mathcal{I}}_{k}}\big(\alpha-{\mathbb{P}}(Y_{n,r,l}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(X_{n,r,l})\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r})\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\left|{{\mathcal{I}}_{k}}\right|}\sum_{l\in{\mathcal{I}}_{k}}\big({\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n,r,i_{k-1}}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(X_{n,r,i_{k-1}})\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}\big)-{\mathbb{P}}(Y_{n,r,l}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(X_{n,r,l}\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}))\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left|{{\mathcal{I}}_{k}}\right|}\sum\limits_{l\in{\mathcal{I}}_{k}}\sum\limits_{{i}={i_{k-1}}}^{l-1}\Big\{{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n,r,i}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(X_{n,r,i})\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}\big)-{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n,r,i+1}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(X_{n,r,i+1})\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}\big)\Big\}

(143)

for any even
k
,
where we invoke the same arguments as in (
71
), albeit with notation adjusted to the full conformal setting.
We observe that, for any
i\in i_{k-1},\ldots,l-1
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n,r,i}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(X_{n,r,i})\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}\big)

\displaystyle-{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n,r,i+1}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(X_{n,r,i+1})\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}\big)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathbbm{1}\{Y_{n,r,i}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(X_{n,r,i})\}\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}\big]-{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathbbm{1}\{Y_{n,r,i+1}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(X_{n,r,i+1})\}\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\sup\limits_{h\in{\mathcal{M}}([0,1])}\Big\{{\mathbb{E}}\big[h(Z_{n,r,i})\big]-{\mathbb{E}}\big[h(Z_{n,r,i+1})\big]\Big\}=\mathsf{TV}\big(Z_{n,r,i},Z_{n,r,i+1}\big),

where
{\mathcal{M}}([0,1])
denotes all measurable functions of
Z\in{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathbb{R}}
that are bounded in
[0,1]
. Accordingly, the bound (
72
) on
A_{k}
in the pretrained-score case can now be replaced by

\displaystyle A_{k}\leq\frac{1}{\left|{{\mathcal{I}}_{k}}\right|}\sum\limits_{l\in{\mathcal{I}}_{k}}\sum\limits_{{i}={i_{k-1}}}^{l-1}\mathsf{TV}(Z_{n,r,i},Z_{n,r,i+1})\leq\sum\limits_{{i}={i_{k-1}}}^{i_{k}-1}\mathsf{TV}(Z_{n,r,i},Z_{n,r,i+1})

for each even
k
.
As a result, under smooth drift, the complexity measure used to control the cumulative regret in stage
n
should now be
\mathsf{TV}_{n}^{\mathsf{stage}}
(cf. (
102
)) rather than
\mathsf{KS}_{n}^{\mathsf{stage}}
(cf. (
40
)).

The remaining arguments are the same as for
Lemmas

B.1
and
B.2
, and are hence omitted for brevity.

C.3.4
Proof of
Lemma

C.4

For any
n
, given that
{\mathcal{B}}_{n}={\mathcal{A}}_{n,r_{n}}\cap{\mathcal{G}}(\tau_{n,r_{n}-1},\tau_{n,r_{n}})
, one has

{\mathbb{E}}\big[\tau_{n+1}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}^{\mathrm{c}}\}\big]\leq{\mathbb{E}}\big[\tau_{n+1}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{A}}_{n,r_{n}}^{\mathrm{c}}\}\big]+{\mathbb{E}}\big[\tau_{n+1}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{G}}(\tau_{n,r_{n}-1},\tau_{n,r_{n}})^{\mathrm{c}}\}\big],

(144)

leaving us with two terms to control.

We first bound
{\mathbb{E}}\big[\tau_{n+1}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{A}}_{n,r_{n}}^{\mathrm{c}}\}\big]
. Recognizing that

n\leq\tau_{n,r_{n}-1}<\tau_{n,r_{n}}
and
\tau_{n+1}\leq 4\tau_{n,r_{n}}
(since the round lengths grow geometrically), we can deduce that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\tau_{n+1}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{A}}_{n,r_{n}}^{\mathrm{c}}\}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{1\leq k<m\leq T}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[4m\,\mathbbm{1}\big\{{\mathcal{A}}_{n,r_{n}}^{\mathrm{c}}\big\}\mathbbm{1}\{\tau_{n,r_{n}-1}=k;~\tau_{n,r_{n}}=m\}\Big]

(145)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{1\leq k<m\leq T}4m{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\mathbbm{1}\{\tau_{n,r_{n}-1}=k;~\tau_{n,r_{n}}=m\}{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathbbm{1}\big\{{\mathcal{A}}_{n,r_{n}}^{\mathrm{c}}\big\}\mid Z_{1:m-1}\big]\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{1\leq k<m\leq T}4m{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\mathbbm{1}\{\tau_{n,r_{n}-1}=k;\tau_{n,r_{n}}=m\}\Big(\sum\limits_{m\leq i<j<\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\big({\mathcal{A}}(k,m;i,j)^{\mathrm{c}}\,|\,Z_{1:m-1}\big)\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:up_bd_A_kmij_c}}{\leq}\sum\limits_{1\leq k<m\leq T}4m{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\mathbbm{1}\{\tau_{n,r_{n}-1}=k;~\tau_{n,r_{n}}=m\}\Big(\sum\limits_{m\leq i<j<\infty}j^{-8}\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{1\leq k<m\leq T}4m{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\mathbbm{1}\{\tau_{n,r_{n}-1}=k;~\tau_{n,r_{n}}=m\}\big(m^{-6}/42\big)\bigg]=\frac{2}{21}{\mathbb{E}}\big[\tau_{n,r_{n}}^{-5}\big]\leq\frac{2}{21n^{5}}.

Next, we turn attention to the term
{\mathbb{E}}\big[\tau_{n+1}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{G}}(\tau_{n,r_{n}-1},\tau_{n,r_{n}})^{\mathrm{c}}\}\big]
.
Using
\tau_{n+1}\leq 16\tau_{n,r_{n}-1}
(which is again due to the geometric growth of the round lengths), we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\tau_{n+1}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{G}}(\tau_{n,r_{n}-1},\tau_{n,r_{n}})^{\mathrm{c}}\}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{{k}={1}}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[16k\,\mathbbm{1}\{\tau_{n,r_{n}-1}=k\}\mathbbm{1}\Big\{{\mathcal{G}}(k,\tau_{n,r_{n}})^{\mathrm{c}}\Big\}\Big]

(146)

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{{k}={1}}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[16k\,\mathbbm{1}\{\tau_{n,r_{n}-1}=k\}\Big(\sum\limits_{{m}={k+1}}^{\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\big({\mathcal{G}}(k,m)^{\mathrm{c}}\,|\,Z_{1:k-1}\big)\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(a)}}{\leq}\sum\limits_{{k}={1}}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[16k\,\mathbbm{1}\{\tau_{n,r_{n}-1}=k\}\Big(\sum_{m=k+1}^{\infty}m^{-4}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{{k}={1}}^{T}6{\mathbb{E}}\Big[k^{-2}\mathbbm{1}\{\tau_{n,r_{n}-1}=k\}\Big]\leq 6{\mathbb{E}}[\tau_{n,r_{n}-1}^{-2}]\leq\frac{6}{n^{2}},

where (a) follows from
Proposition

4.1
with
\delta=m^{-4}
.

Taking together (
144
)–(
146
) thus completes the proof.

C.3.5
Proof of
Lemma

C.5

Recall the algorithm procedure of
DriftOCP
-
full
: in stage
n~(\leq N-1)
, the distribution shift is detected in round
r_{n}
, and this round contains
t_{n}
iterations. Then in light of our drift detection subroutine (see Algorithm
3
), there exists some
j_{n}\in[t_{n}]
such that:

\Bigg|\sum\limits_{{l}={j_{n}}}^{t_{n}}\big(\mathbbm{1}\{Y_{n,r_{n},l}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r_{n}}(X_{n,r_{n},l})\}-\alpha\big)\Bigg|>10\sqrt{t_{n}-j_{n}+1}\log^{3}(40\tau_{n,r_{n}}).

Then on the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{n,r_{n}}
(cf. (
96
)), it holds that

\displaystyle\bigg|\,\sum\limits_{{l}={j_{n}}}^{t_{n}}
