\displaystyle\big({\mathbb{P}}(Y_{n,r_{n},l}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r_{n}}(X_{n,r_{n},l})\,|\,{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r_{n}})-\alpha\big)\,\bigg|\geq\Bigg|\sum\limits_{{l}={j_{n}}}^{t_{n}}\big(\mathbbm{1}\{Y_{n,r_{n},l}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r_{n}}(X_{n,r_{n},l})\}-\alpha\big)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad-\Bigg|\sum\limits_{{l}={j_{n}}}^{t_{n}}\Big(\mathbbm{1}\big\{Y_{n,r_{n},l}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r_{n}}(X_{n,r_{n},l})\big\}-{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n,r_{n},l}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r_{n}}(X_{n,r_{n},l})\,|\,{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r_{n}}\big)\Big)\Bigg|

\displaystyle>10\sqrt{t_{n}-j_{n}+1}\log^{3}(40\tau_{n,r_{n}})-2\sqrt{(t_{n}-j_{n}+1)\log(2\tau_{n+1,1})}

\displaystyle\geq 8\sqrt{t_{n}-j_{n}+1}\log^{3}(40\tau_{n,r_{n}}).

(147)

As a consequence, if we define

\displaystyle B_{n}\coloneqq\frac{1}{t_{n}-j_{n}+1}\sum\limits_{{l}={j_{n}}}^{t_{n}}\Big({\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n,r_{n},l}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r_{n}}(X_{n,r_{n},l})\,|\,{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r_{n}}\big)-\alpha\Big),

(148)

then (
147
) implies that

\frac{\sqrt{t_{n}-j_{n}+1}}{8\log^{3}(40\tau_{n,r_{n}})}\left|{B_{n}}\right|\geq 1.

(149)

The next step is to analyze the quantity
B_{n}
defined in (
148
). Note that
\tau_{n,r_{n}}-\tau_{n,r_{n}-1}=T_{r_{n}-1}
. Then on the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{n}
defined in (
104
)—more precisely, on the event
{\mathcal{G}}(\tau_{n,r_{n}-1},\tau_{n,r_{n}})
defined in (
103
)—we have

\displaystyle\left|{B_{n}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n,r_{n},1}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r_{n}}(X_{n,r_{n},1})\,|\,{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r_{n}}\big)-\alpha\,\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{t_{n}-j_{n}+1}\sum\limits_{{l}={j_{n}}}^{t_{n}}\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n,r_{n},l}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r_{n}}(X_{n,r_{n},l})\,|\,{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r_{n}}\big)-{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n,r_{n},1}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r_{n}}(X_{n,r_{n},1})\,|\,{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r_{n}}\big)\,\Big|

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(a)}}{\leq}\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n,r_{n},1}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r_{n}}(X_{n,r_{n},1})\,|\,{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r_{n}}\big)-\alpha\,\Big|+\frac{1}{t_{n}-j_{n}+1}\sum\limits_{{l}={j_{n}}}^{t_{n}}\mathsf{TV}(Z_{n,r_{n},l},Z_{n,r_{n},1})

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(b)}}{\leq}\frac{1}{t_{n}-j_{n}+1}\sum\limits_{{l}={j_{n}}}^{t_{n}}\left(2^{6}\sqrt{\frac{\log(40\tau_{n,r_{n}})}{T_{r_{n}-1}}}+\frac{2^{7}{L}}{\tau_{n,r_{n}}}\sqrt{T_{r_{n}-1}\log(40\tau_{n,r_{n}})}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{t_{n}-j_{n}+1}\sum\limits_{{l}={j_{n}}}^{t_{n}}\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{l-1}\mathsf{TV}\big(Z_{n,r_{n},j},Z_{n,r_{n},j+1}\big)+\frac{1}{T_{r_{n}-1}}\sum\limits_{{l}={1}}^{T_{r_{n}-1}}\sum\limits_{{i}={l}}^{T_{r_{n}-1}}\mathsf{TV}\big(Z_{n,r_{n}-1,i},Z_{n,r_{n}-1,i+1}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{6}\sqrt{\frac{\log(40\tau_{n,r_{n}})}{T_{r_{n}-1}}}+\frac{2^{7}{L}}{\tau_{n,r_{n}}}\sqrt{T_{r_{n}-1}\log(40\tau_{n,r_{n}})}+\mathsf{TV}_{n}^{\mathsf{tail}},

(150)

where (b) is valid on
{\mathcal{B}}_{n}
, and (a) results from the fact that, for any
l=j_{n},\ldots,t_{n}
,

\displaystyle\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n,r,l}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(X_{n,r,l})\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}\big)

\displaystyle-{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n,r,1}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(X_{n,r,1})\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}\big)\,\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\,{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathbbm{1}\{Y_{n,r,l}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(X_{n,r,l})\}\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}\big]-{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathbbm{1}\{Y_{n,r,1}\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}(X_{n,r,1})\}\mid{\mathcal{C}}_{n,r}\big]\,\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup\limits_{h\in{\mathcal{M}}([0,1])}\Big\{{\mathbb{E}}\big[h(Z_{n,r,l})\big]-{\mathbb{E}}\big[h(Z_{n,r,1})\big]\Big\}=\mathsf{TV}\big(Z_{n,r,l},Z_{n,r,1}\big),

where
{\mathcal{M}}([0,1])
denotes the set of all measurable functions
h:{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathbb{R}}\to[0,1]
.

Regarding the first term on the right-hand side of (
150
),
one can apply (
149
) together with a little algebra to show that: when
\tau_{n,r_{n}-1}\geq 2
:

\displaystyle 2^{6}\sqrt{\frac{\log(40\tau_{n,r_{n}})}{T_{r_{n}-1}}}\overset{\eqref{eq:full_regret_3}}{\leq}\frac{2^{6}}{8\log^{5/2}(40\tau_{n,r_{n}})}\sqrt{\frac{t_{n}-j_{n}+1}{T_{r_{n}-1}}}\left|{B_{n}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{2}\left|{B_{n}}\right|.

(151)

Combine this with (
150
) and rearrange terms to reach

\left|{B_{n}}\right|\leq\frac{2^{8}{L}}{\tau_{n,r_{n}}}\sqrt{T_{r_{n}-1}\log(40\tau_{n,r_{n}})}+2\mathsf{TV}_{n}^{\mathsf{tail}}.

To finish up, recall that
{\mathcal{H}}_{n}\coloneqq\left\{{T_{r_{n}-1}\sqrt{\log(40\tau_{n,r_{n}})}}\leq\frac{\tau_{n,r_{n}}}{256{L}}\right\}
. On the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{n}\cap{\mathcal{H}}_{n}
, combining the above expression with (
149
) allows us to establish the following inequality:

\displaystyle 2+2\sqrt{t_{n}-j_{n}+1}\,\mathsf{TV}_{n}^{\mathsf{tail}}

\displaystyle\overset{{\mathcal{H}}_{n}}{\geq}\sqrt{t_{n}-j_{n}+1}\left(\frac{2^{8}{L}}{\tau_{n,r_{n}}}\sqrt{T_{r_{n}-1}\log(40\tau_{n,r_{n}})}+2\mathsf{TV}_{n}^{\mathsf{tail}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{t_{n}-j_{n}+1}\left|{B_{n}}\right|\overset{\eqref{eq:full_regret_3}}{\geq}8,

which in turn implies that

\sqrt{t_{n}-j_{n}+1}\,\mathsf{TV}_{n}^{\mathsf{tail}}\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}\cap{\mathcal{H}}_{n}\}\geq 3\cdot\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{B}}_{n}\cap{\mathcal{H}}_{n}\}.

(152)

This immediately concludes the proof of this lemma.

C.4
Proof of Theorem
4.2

Throughout this subsection, we consider the case where no features
\{X_{t}\}_{t=1}^{T}
are observed; instead, only the response
Y_{t}
is available at time
t
. Under this simplification, the set-valued functions
\{{\mathcal{C}}_{t}(\cdot)\}_{t\geq 1}
induced by algorithm
\pi=\{\pi_{t}\}_{t\geq 1}
in (
32
) admit the simpler representation

{\mathcal{C}}_{t}=\begin{cases}\pi_{1}(U),&\text{if }t=1,\\
\pi_{t}(Y_{t-1},\ldots,Y_{1},U),&\text{if }t\geq 2,\end{cases}

(153)

where

\pi_{t}:{\mathbb{R}}^{t-1}\times[0,1]\to{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{R}})
for
t\geq 2
.
In this setting,
{\mathcal{C}}_{t}
is uniquely determined by
Y_{1:t-1}
and
U
; accordingly, we often write it as
{\mathcal{C}}(Y_{1:t-1},U)
as long as it is clear from the context.

Our proof of Theorem
4.2
is organized into several steps, presented below.

Step 1: constructing a class of distributions with piecewise flat density.

We begin by introducing the distribution class
{\mathcal{I}}
, constructed as follows.

•

First, divide the interval
[0,1]
into
k
subintervals:

I_{j}\coloneqq\left[\frac{j-1}{k},\frac{j}{k}\right),\quad j=1,\ldots,k-1;\quad I_{k}\coloneqq\left[\frac{k-1}{k},1\right].

(154)

•

For any given sequence
V_{1},\dots,V_{k}\in\{-1,1\}
, generate a distribution with probability density function

\displaystyle f(y\mid V_{1:k})\coloneqq\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}f_{j}(y),

(155a)

where
f_{j}(\cdot)
is nonzero only within the subinterval
I_{j}
as follows:

f_{j}(y)\propto(1+\epsilon V_{j})\,\mathbbm{1}_{I_{j}}(y),

with
\epsilon
a small positive constant to be specified shortly, and

\mathbbm{1}_{I_{j}}(y)\coloneqq\mathbbm{1}\{y\in I_{j}\}
the indicator function of
I_{j}
.
The normalization constant can then be computed as

\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\int_{I_{j}}(1+\epsilon V_{j})\,{\textnormal{d}}y=\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\frac{1+\epsilon V_{j}}{k}\eqqcolon 1+\epsilon\overline{V},

thereby allowing us to express

\displaystyle f_{j}(y)=\frac{(1+\epsilon V_{j})\mathbbm{1}_{I_{j}}(y)}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}},\quad j=1,2,\ldots,k.

(155b)

•

Accordingly, we construct a distribution class as follows

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}\coloneqq\left\{\,f(y\mid V_{1:k})\,\big|\,V_{1:k}\in\{-1,1\}^{k}\,\right\}.

(156)

Step 2: constructing a family of distribution sequences contained in
\mathcal{L}_{3}(N^{\mathsf{cp}})
and
\mathcal{L}_{4}(\mathsf{TV}_{T})
.

With
\mathcal{I}
in place, we would like to construct a family
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}
of distribution sequences such that it is composed of all
\{\mathcal{D}_{t}\}_{t=1}^{T}
satisfying the following two conditions:

1.

{\mathcal{D}}_{t}\in{\mathcal{I}}\text{ for every }t=1,\ldots,T
;

2.

For every
l=1,\ldots,m+1
, it holds that
{\mathcal{D}}_{t}={\mathcal{D}}_{t+1}
for any
t
obeying

(l-1)\lfloor T/m\rfloor+1\leq t<\min\left\{l\lfloor T/m\rfloor,T\right\}
.
In other words, the distributions are identical within each segment

{\mathcal{T}}_{l}\coloneqq\big[(l-1)\lfloor T/m\rfloor+1,\min\left\{l\lfloor T/m\rfloor,T\right\}\big],

(157)

where each batch
\mathcal{T}_{l}
(except for the
(m+1)
-th batch) contains
\lfloor T/m\rfloor
time instances.

We now verify that
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
belongs to both
\mathcal{L}_{3}(N^{\mathsf{cp}})
and
\mathcal{L}_{4}(\mathsf{TV}_{T})
under an appropriate choice of parameters.

•

Regarding the change-point setting, it is clearly seen that
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{L}_{3}(N^{\mathsf{cp}})
when
m=N^{\mathsf{cp}}
.

•

Turning to the smooth drift setting, we claim that
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{L}}_{4}(\mathsf{TV}_{T})
for sufficiently small
\epsilon
.
To justify this, consider any two distributions
{\mathcal{D}}_{1},{\mathcal{D}}_{2}\in{\mathcal{I}}
, and suppose that their densities can be written as

p_{{\mathcal{D}}_{i}}(y)=f(y\mid V_{1:k}^{(i)})
for
i=1,2
. Assuming that
\epsilon\leq 1/2
, we can calculate

\displaystyle\mathsf{TV}({\mathcal{D}}_{1},{\mathcal{D}}_{2})=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\left|{f_{1}(y\mid V_{1:k}^{(1)})-f_{1}(y\mid V_{1:k}^{(2)})}\right|{\textnormal{d}}y=\frac{1}{2}\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\int_{I_{j}}\left|{\frac{1+\epsilon V_{j}^{(1)}}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}^{(1)}}-\frac{1+\epsilon V_{j}^{(2)}}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}^{(2)}}}\right|{\textnormal{d}}y

(158)

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\frac{2}{k}\left|{\epsilon\big(V_{j}^{(1)}+\overline{V}^{(2)}-V_{j}^{(2)}-\overline{V}^{(1)}\big)+\epsilon^{2}\big(V_{j}^{(1)}\overline{V}^{(2)}-V_{j}^{(2)}\overline{V}^{(1)}\big)}\right|\leq\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\frac{2}{k}(4\epsilon+2\epsilon^{2})\leq 10\epsilon,

where the inequalities in the last line result from
\epsilon\leq 1/2
and
\max\limits_{j\in[k],i=1,2}\{|V_{j}^{(i)}|,|\overline{V}^{(i)}|\}\leq 1
. As a result, if we take
\epsilon\leq\min\{\mathsf{TV}_{T}/(20m),1/2\}
, then for any
\{{\mathcal{D}}_{t}\}_{t=1}^{T}
we have

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{{t}={1}}^{T-1}\mathsf{TV}({\mathcal{D}}_{t},{\mathcal{D}}_{t+1})

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{m}\mathsf{TV}\left({\mathcal{D}}_{(j-1)\lfloor\frac{T}{m}\rfloor+1},{\mathcal{D}}_{j\lfloor\frac{T}{m}\rfloor+1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq m\sup\limits_{{\mathcal{D}}_{1},{\mathcal{D}}_{2}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\mathsf{TV}({\mathcal{D}}_{1},{\mathcal{D}}_{2})\overset{\eqref{eq:lower_bd_1}}{\leq}20m\epsilon\leq\mathsf{TV}_{T},

thus ensuring that
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{L}}_{4}(\mathsf{TV}_{T})
.

Step 3: establishing a general regret lower bound.

We now look at the cumulative regret within each batch
{\mathcal{T}}_{i}
(
i=1,\ldots,m+1
) defined in (
157
).
To this end, we first establish the following lower bound on the coverage gap for a single time point; the proof is deferred to
Section

C.4.1
.

Lemma C.6
.

Suppose that
0<\alpha\leq 1/2
,
k\geq\frac{256K}{\alpha}
and
\epsilon\leq\min\left\{\frac{\alpha^{5/2}}{200},\frac{1}{64}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha k}{n\log(2nk/\alpha^{2})}}\right\}
.
Consider any
n\geq 1
.
Then, for any admissible algorithm
\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{K}
(cf. (
33
)), the set-value mapping
{\mathcal{C}}
induced by
\pi
satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|{{\mathcal{I}}}\right|}\sum\limits_{{\mathcal{D}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\Big\{\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Y_{1:n}\sim{\mathcal{D}}^{n},\,U\sim p_{U}}\big[\big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n+1}\in{\mathcal{C}}(Y_{1:n},U)\,|\,Y_{1:n},U\big)-(1-\alpha)\,\big|\big]\Big\}\geq\frac{\alpha^{\frac{5}{2}}\epsilon}{144\sqrt{k}}-\frac{\alpha^{6}}{4n^{3}k^{3}},

(159)

where
U
is independently drawn from an arbitrary continuous distribution with density function
p_{U}(\cdot)
, and
\mathcal{I}
is defined in Step 1 (with the parameter
\epsilon
).

With this intermediate result in hand, we can now analyze the cumulative coverage gap within each batch
{\mathcal{T}}_{i}
(cf. (
157
)). In fact, letting
\tau_{i}\coloneqq(i-1)\left\lfloor T/m\right\rfloor+1
, one can write

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}_{i}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{t}\in{\mathcal{C}}(Y_{1:t-1},U)

\displaystyle\,|\,Y_{1:t-1},U\big)-(1-\alpha)\,\big|\Big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}_{i}}{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\Big(Y_{t}\in{\mathcal{C}}(Y_{1:\tau_{i}-1},Y_{\tau_{i}:t-1},U)\,\big|\,Y_{1:t-1},U\Big)-(1-\alpha)\,\Big|\bigg].

Note that by construction, the distribution selected for each batch is independent of the distributions assigned to all preceding batches.
Therefore, we can view
(Y_{1:\tau_{i}-1},U)
jointly as a new random variable

\widetilde{U}\sim P_{\widetilde{u}}
for some distribution
P_{\widetilde{u}}
, which is independent of all randomness within
{\mathcal{T}}_{i}
.
Consequently, we shall write the prediction set

{\mathcal{C}}(Y_{1:\tau_{i}-1},Y_{\tau_{i}:t-1},U)

as
{\mathcal{C}}(Y_{\tau_{i}:t-1},\widetilde{U})
in the sequel (as long as it is clear from the context), in order to underscore the role of
Y_{\tau_{i}:t-1}
.
Armed with this simplified notation, we define,
for any given distribution
{\mathcal{D}}
and any index
i\in[m+1]
, the cumulative regret over batch
\mathcal{T}_{i}
as

\displaystyle\mathsf{regret}_{\pi}({\mathcal{D}},{\mathcal{T}}_{i})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum\limits_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}_{i}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|{{\mathbb{P}}\Big(Y_{t}\in{\mathcal{C}}(Y_{1:\tau_{i}-1},Y_{\tau_{i}:t-1},U)\,\big|\,Y_{1:t-1},U\Big)-(1-\alpha)}\right|\right]

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}_{i}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left|{{\mathbb{P}}\Big(Y_{t}\in{\mathcal{C}}(Y_{\tau_{i}:t-1},\widetilde{U})\,\big|\,Y_{\tau_{i}:t-1},\widetilde{U}\Big)-(1-\alpha)}\right|\right].

Applying Lemma
C.6
for each time
t
, we reach

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|{{\mathcal{I}}}\right|}\sum\limits_{{\mathcal{D}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\mathsf{regret}_{\pi}({\mathcal{D}},{\mathcal{T}}_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left|{{\mathcal{I}}}\right|}\sum\limits_{{\mathcal{D}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\sum\limits_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}_{i}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\left|{{\mathbb{P}}\Big(Y_{t}\in{\mathcal{C}}(Y_{\tau_{i}:t-1},\widetilde{U})\,\big|\,Y_{\tau_{i}:t-1},\widetilde{U}\Big)-(1-\alpha)}\right|\Big]

(160)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}_{i}}\left\{\frac{1}{\left|{{\mathcal{I}}}\right|}\sum\limits_{{\mathcal{D}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\left|{{\mathbb{P}}\Big(Y_{t}\in{\mathcal{C}}(Y_{\tau_{i}:t-1},\widetilde{U})\,\big|\,Y_{\tau_{i},t-1},\widetilde{U}\Big)-(1-\alpha)}\right|\Big]\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\sum\limits_{t\in{\mathcal{T}}_{i}}\left(\frac{\alpha^{\frac{5}{2}}\epsilon}{144\sqrt{k}}-\frac{\alpha^{6}}{4(T/m)^{3}k^{3}}\right)=\left(\frac{\alpha^{\frac{5}{2}}\epsilon}{144\sqrt{k}}-\frac{\alpha^{6}}{4(T/m)^{3}k^{3}}\right)|{\mathcal{T}}_{i}|,

provided that

\displaystyle\epsilon\leq\min\left\{\frac{\alpha^{\frac{5}{2}}}{200},\frac{1}{64}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha mk}{T\log(Tk/\alpha m)}}\right\}.

(161)

Now, putting all batches together yields the following regret lower bound: for any algorithm
\pi\in\mathcal{P}_{K}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{regret}_{\pi}({\mathcal{L}}^{\prime},T,K)

\displaystyle=\sup\limits_{\{{\mathcal{D}}_{t}\}_{t=1}^{T}\in{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}}\mathsf{regret}_{\pi}\big({\mathcal{D}}_{1:T},T\big)=\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m+1}\sup\limits_{{\mathcal{D}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\mathsf{regret}_{\pi}({\mathcal{D}},{\mathcal{T}}_{i})

(162)

\displaystyle\geq\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{m+1}\frac{1}{\left|{{\mathcal{I}}}\right|}\sum\limits_{{\mathcal{D}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\mathsf{regret}_{\pi}({\mathcal{D}},{\mathcal{T}}_{i})\overset{\eqref{eq:one_seg_lower_bound_full}}{\geq}\left(\frac{\alpha^{\frac{5}{2}}\epsilon}{144\sqrt{k}}-\frac{\alpha^{6}}{4n^{3}k^{3}}\right)T.

Step 4: instantiating the general lower bound to two drift settings.

It remains to connect the above lower bound to the two distribution-drift settings, which we discuss separately below.

•

The change-point setting.

In this drift scenario, setting
m=N^{\mathsf{cp}}
ensures that
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{L}_{3}(N^{\mathsf{cp}})
(as discussed in Step 2). Then, taking
k=\frac{256K}{\alpha}
and
\epsilon=\min\Big\{\frac{\alpha^{5/2}}{200},\frac{1}{64}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha k(N^{\mathsf{cp}}+1)}{T\log(Tk/\alpha)}}\Big\}
in (
162
) yields

\displaystyle\mathsf{regret}_{\pi}\big({\mathcal{L}}_{3}(N^{\mathsf{cp}}),T,K\big)\geq\mathsf{regret}_{\pi}\big({\mathcal{L}}^{\prime},T,K\big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{300}\min\Bigg\{\frac{\alpha^{5}T}{200\sqrt{k}},\frac{\alpha^{3}}{16}\sqrt{\frac{(N^{\mathsf{cp}}+1)T}{\log(Tk/\alpha)}}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Omega}\Bigg(\min\left\{\frac{T}{\sqrt{K}},\sqrt{(N^{\mathsf{cp}}+1)T}\right\}\Bigg).

•

The smooth drift setting.

In order to simultaneously satisfy (
161
) and
{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{L}}_{4}(\mathsf{TV}_{T})
(which needs
\epsilon\leq\min\{\mathsf{TV}_{T}/(20m),1/2\}
as discussed in Step 2), we take
\epsilon
to be

\epsilon=\min\left\{\frac{\alpha^{\frac{5}{2}}}{200},\frac{\mathsf{TV}_{T}}{20m},\frac{1}{64}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha mk}{T\log(Tk/\alpha m)}}\right\}.

Substitution into inequality (
162
) leads to

\mathsf{regret}_{\pi}\big({\mathcal{L}}_{4}(\mathsf{TV}_{T}),T,K\big)\geq\mathsf{regret}_{\pi}({\mathcal{L}}^{\prime},T,K)\geq\frac{\alpha^{\frac{5}{2}}T}{300\sqrt{k}}\min\left\{\frac{\alpha^{\frac{5}{2}}}{200},\frac{\mathsf{TV}_{T}}{20m},\frac{1}{64}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha mk}{T\log(Tk/\alpha m)}}\right\}.

(163)

We now divide into two cases.

–

If
\mathsf{TV}_{T}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha T}{12K}}\geq 1
,
then let us take
m=\frac{\mathsf{TV}_{T}^{\frac{2}{3}}T^{\frac{1}{3}}\log^{\frac{2}{3}}(Tk/\alpha)}{(\alpha k)^{\frac{1}{3}}}
and
k=\frac{256K}{\alpha}
, giving rise to

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha^{\frac{5}{2}}T}{300\sqrt{k}}\min\left\{\frac{\alpha^{\frac{5}{2}}}{200},\frac{\mathsf{TV}_{T}}{20m},\sqrt{\frac{\alpha mk}{2^{8}T\log(Tk/\alpha m)}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Omega}\Bigg(\frac{T}{\sqrt{k}}\min\bigg\{1,\Big(kT^{-1}\mathsf{TV}_{T}\Big)^{\frac{1}{3}}\bigg\}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Omega}\left(\min\left\{\frac{T}{\sqrt{K}},\frac{\mathsf{TV}_{T}^{\frac{1}{3}}T^{\frac{2}{3}}}{K^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right\}\right).

Plugging this into (
163
) yields

\mathsf{regret}_{\pi}\big({\mathcal{L}}_{4}(\mathsf{TV}_{T}),T,K\big)\geq\widetilde{\Omega}\left(\min\left\{\frac{T}{\sqrt{K}},\frac{\mathsf{TV}_{T}^{\frac{1}{3}}T^{\frac{2}{3}}}{K^{\frac{1}{6}}}\right\}\right)

(164)

–

Next, consider the case with
\mathsf{TV}_{T}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha T}{12K}}<1
. Note that for any
{\mathcal{D}}\in{\mathcal{I}}
, the constant distribution sequence
{\mathcal{D}}_{1:T}
with
{\mathcal{D}}_{1}=\cdots={\mathcal{D}}_{T}={\mathcal{D}}
belongs to
{\mathcal{L}}_{4}(\mathsf{TV}_{T})
, since its cumulative variation equals
0
.
Then one can apply
Lemma

C.6
with
\epsilon=\min\left\{\frac{\alpha^{5/2}}{200},\sqrt{\frac{\alpha K}{2^{8}T\log(TK/\alpha)}}\right\}
and
k=\frac{256}{\alpha}K
to the entire horizon
[T]
to arrive at

\displaystyle\mathsf{regret}_{\pi}\big({\mathcal{L}}_{4}(\mathsf{TV}_{T}),T,K\big)

\displaystyle\geq\sup\limits_{{\mathcal{D}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\mathsf{regret}_{\pi}({\mathcal{D}}^{T},T,K)\geq\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{{\mathcal{D}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\bigg[\sum\limits_{{t}={1}}^{T}\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{t}\in{\mathcal{C}}(Y_{1:t-1})\big)-(1-\alpha)\,\Big|\bigg]

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Omega}\bigg(\min\left\{\frac{T}{\sqrt{K}},\sqrt{T}\right\}\bigg).

(165)

Combining the bounds (
164
) and (
165
)
establishes the desired lower bound for the smooth drift setting.

The proof of Theorem
4.2
is thus complete.

C.4.1
Proof of Lemma
C.6

Throughout this proof, we shall often write
{\mathcal{C}}(Y_{1:n},U)
simply as
{\mathcal{C}}
, as long as it is clear from the context.
For each
j=1,\ldots,k
, we define

\displaystyle a_{j}=a_{j}(Y_{1:n},U)\coloneqq\mu({\mathcal{C}}\cap I_{j}),

(166a)

where we often abbreviate
a_{j}(Y_{1:n},U)
as
a_{j}
.
Here,
I_{j}
is given in (
154
) and
\mu(\cdot)
denotes the Lebesgue measure on the interval
[0,1]
.
Further, let

\overline{a}\coloneqq\frac{1}{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}a_{j}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\widetilde{a}_{j}\coloneqq a_{j}-\overline{a}~~(j=1,\ldots,k).

(166b)

The following lemma characterizes the range of
\sum_{j=1}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}
, which will be used repeatedly in our analysis. Its proof is deferred to
Section

C.4.2
.

Lemma C.7
.

For any given realization of
Y_{1:n},U
, it always holds that

\frac{1}{k}\bigg(\mu({\mathcal{C}})\big(1-\mu({\mathcal{C}})\big)-\frac{2K}{k}\bigg)_{+}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}\leq\frac{\mu({\mathcal{C}})\big(1-\mu({\mathcal{C}})\big)}{k}\leq\frac{1}{4k}.

We now embark on the proof, which contains a few steps below.

Step 1: an expression for the training-conditional coverage gap.

Under the distribution
{\mathcal{D}}
with parameters
V_{1:k}
(see (
155
)), we can derive

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n+1}\in{\mathcal{C}}\,|\,Y_{1:n},U\big)

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\int\frac{1+\epsilon V_{j}}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}}\mathbbm{1}\{y\in{\mathcal{C}}\cap I_{j}\}{\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\frac{1+\epsilon V_{j}}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}}a_{j}=\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}a_{j}+\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}}\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}(V_{j}-\overline{V})a_{j}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(a)}}{=}\mu({\mathcal{C}})+\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}}\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}(V_{j}-\overline{V})a_{j}=\mu({\mathcal{C}})+\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}}\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}V_{j}\widetilde{a}_{j},

where (a) follows since the sets
\{{\mathcal{C}}\cap I_{j}\}_{j=1}^{k}
are mutually disjoint and the last equality results from

\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\overline{V}a_{j}=k\overline{V}\frac{1}{k}\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}a_{j}=\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}V_{j}\overline{a}.

Note that each distribution
{\mathcal{D}}
in
{\mathcal{I}}
(cf. (
156
)) is uniquely determined by the sequence
V_{1:k}\in\{\pm 1\}^{k}
.
To make the subsequent analysis clearer and more concise,
we define

l(V_{1:k},{\mathcal{C}})\coloneqq\left|{{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n+1}\in{\mathcal{C}}\,|\,Y_{1:n},U)-(1-\alpha)}\right|,

which, according to the above calculation, can be written as

l(V_{1:k},{\mathcal{C}})=\bigg|\mu({\mathcal{C}})-(1-\alpha)+\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}}\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}V_{j}\widetilde{a}_{j}\bigg|.

(167)

As can be easily seen, averaging over
{\mathcal{D}}\in{\mathcal{I}}
on the left-hand side of (
159
) is equivalent to taking expectation with respect to
V_{1:k}
drawn from the uniform distribution
{\mathcal{D}}_{V}^{k}\coloneqq\mathsf{Unif}\big(\{\pm 1\}^{k}\big)
, namely,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|{{\mathcal{I}}}\right|}\sum\limits_{{\mathcal{D}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Y_{1:n}\sim{\mathcal{D}}^{n},\,U\sim p_{U}}

\displaystyle\Big[\big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n+1}\in{\mathcal{C}}(Y_{1:n},U)\,|\,Y_{1:n},U\big)-(1-\alpha)\,\big|\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{V_{1:k}\sim{\mathcal{D}}_{V}^{k}}\Big[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{{\mathcal{D}}^{n}\times p_{U}}\big[\left|{{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n+1}\in{\mathcal{C}}\,|\,Y_{1:n},U\big)-(1-\alpha)}\right|\big]\Big].

(168)

By fully expanding the right-hand side of (
168
) w.r.t. the generative process of
U,~V_{1:k}
and
Y_{1:n}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{V_{1:k}\sim{\mathcal{D}}_{V}^{k}}\Big[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{{\mathcal{D}}^{n}\times p_{U}}\big[\big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n+1}\in{\mathcal{C}}

\displaystyle\,|\,Y_{1:n},U\big)-(1-\alpha)\,\big|\big]\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{k}}\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}\Bigg\{\int_{[0,1]^{n}\times{\mathcal{U}}}l(V_{1:k},{\mathcal{C}})\bigg(\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}f(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})\bigg)p_{U}(u){\textnormal{d}}y_{1:n}{\textnormal{d}}u\Bigg\},

(169)

where
\mathcal{U}
is the support of
U
, and the summation ranges over all
2^{k}
choices of
V_{1:k}
in
\{\pm 1\}^{k}
.
Denote by

\displaystyle N_{j}(y_{1:n})=\left|{\{i\mid i\in[n],y_{i}\in I_{j}\}}\right|

(170)

the number of responses that fall in the interval
I_{j}
; when there is no ambiguity, we abbreviate
N_{j}(y_{1:n})
as
N_{j}
.
This allows us to express the joint density as

\prod_{i=1}^{n}f(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})=\prod_{j=1}^{k}\left(\frac{1+\epsilon V_{j}}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}}\right)^{N_{j}}=\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{k}(1+\epsilon V_{j})^{N_{j}}}{\big(1+\epsilon\overline{V}\big)^{n}},

which combined with (
169
) yields an expression for the expected training-conditional coverage gap:

\displaystyle\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{V_{1:k}\sim{\mathcal{D}}_{V}^{k}}\Big[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{{\mathcal{D}}^{n}\times p_{U}}\big[\big|{\mathbb{P}}(Y_{n+1}\in{\mathcal{C}}\mid Y_{1:n},U)-(1-\alpha)\big|\big]\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}\Bigg\{\int\limits_{[0,1]^{n}\times{\mathcal{U}}}\underset{\eqqcolon\,p(V_{1:k},y_{1:n},u)}{\underbrace{\frac{p_{U}(u)\prod_{j=1}^{k}(1+\epsilon V_{j})^{N_{j}(y_{1:n})}}{2^{k}(1+\epsilon\overline{V})^{n}}}}l(V_{1:k},{\mathcal{C}}){\textnormal{d}}y_{1:n}{\textnormal{d}}u\Bigg\}.

(171)

Here, the term
p(V_{1:k},y_{1:n},u)
represents the joint density of the random tuple
(V_{1:k},Y_{1:n},U)
.

Step 2: a lower bound on the coverage gap using auxiliary conditional distributions.

Now, consider the conditional distribution of
V_{1:k}
given
y_{1:n}
and
u
, denoted by
p(V_{1:k}\mid y_{1:n},u)
.
As it turns out, one can construct a set of auxiliary conditional densities

q_{j}(\cdot\mid y_{1:n},u)
for
j=1,\ldots,k
,
whose product provides a good approximation to
p(V_{1:k}\mid y_{1:n},u)
. This is formalized in the following lemma, whose proof is given in
Section

C.4.3
.

Lemma C.8
.

For any
y_{1:n}
and
u
, let
p(y_{1:n},u)\coloneqq\sum_{v_{1:k}}p(v_{1:k},y_{1:n},u)
.
There exists a collection of conditional distributions

\{q_{j}(V_{j}\mid y_{1:n},u)\}_{j=1}^{k}
such that

\displaystyle\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{V_{1:k}\sim{\mathcal{D}}_{V}^{k}}\Big[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{{\mathcal{D}}^{n}\times p_{U}}\big[\big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n+1}\in{\mathcal{C}}\,|\,Y_{1:n},U\big)

\displaystyle-(1-\alpha)\,\big|\big]\Big]

(172)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{3}\int\limits_{[0,1]^{n}\times{\mathcal{U}}}{\mathcal{L}}_{Q}(y_{1:n},u)p(y_{1:n},u){\textnormal{d}}y_{1:n}{\textnormal{d}}u-\frac{\alpha^{6}}{4n^{3}k^{3}}

as long as
\epsilon\leq\sqrt{\frac{k}{12n\log(2nk/\alpha^{2})}}
, where

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{Q}(y_{1:n},u)\coloneqq\sum_{V_{1:k}}\bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{k}q_{j}(V_{j}\mid y_{1:n},u)\bigg)\,l(V_{1:k},{\mathcal{C}}).

(173)

As can be seen, each summand in
{\mathcal{L}}_{Q}(y_{1:n},u)
(cf. (
173
)) involves the product distribution based on
\{q_{j}(\cdot\mid y_{1:n},u)\}
.

Step 3: a decomposition of
{\mathcal{L}}_{Q}(y_{1:n},u)
.

With Lemma
C.8
, we now turn to the analysis of the quantity
{\mathcal{L}}_{Q}(y_{1:n},u)
(cf. (
173
)).
Given
(y_{1:n},u)
, define
{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}
as the product distribution

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}(V_{1:k})\coloneqq\prod_{j=1}^{k}q_{j}(V_{j}\mid y_{1:n},u),

(174)

and draw an independent copy

V_{1:k}^{\prime}
of
V_{1:k}
from the same distribution

{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}
.
Then, invoking (
167
) and the triangle inequality, we can derive

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{Q}(y_{1:n},u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{V_{1:k}}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{k}q_{j}(V_{j}\mid y_{1:n},u)\Big)\,l(V_{1:k},{\mathcal{C}})={\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}}\big[l(V_{1:k},{\mathcal{C}})\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}}\big[l(V_{1:k},{\mathcal{C}})\big]+\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}}\big[l(V_{1:k}^{\prime},{\mathcal{C}})\big]

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:def_l_VC}}{=}\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}^{2}}\left[\left|{\big(\mu({\mathcal{C}})-(1-\alpha)\big)+\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}}\left\langle V_{1:k},\widetilde{a}_{1:k}\right\rangle}\right|+\left|{\big(\mu({\mathcal{C}})-(1-\alpha)\big)+\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}^{\prime}}\left\langle V_{1:k}^{\prime},\widetilde{a}_{1:k}\right\rangle}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}^{2}}\left[\left|{\left(\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}}\left\langle V_{1:k},\widetilde{a}_{1:k}\right\rangle-\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}}\left\langle V_{1:k}^{\prime},\widetilde{a}_{1:k}\right\rangle\right)+\left(\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}}-\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}^{\prime}}\right)\left\langle V_{1:k}^{\prime},\widetilde{a}_{1:k}\right\rangle}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}^{2}}\left[\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}}\left|{\left\langle V_{1:k}-V_{1:k}^{\prime},\widetilde{a}_{1:k}\right\rangle}\right|\right]-\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}^{2}}\left[\left|{\left(\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}}-\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}^{\prime}}\right)\left\langle V_{1:k}^{\prime},\widetilde{a}_{1:k}\right\rangle}\right|\right],

from which it follows that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\big[{\mathcal{L}}_{Q}(Y_{1:n},U)\big]

\displaystyle\geq\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}^{2}}\left[\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}}\left|{\left\langle V_{1:k}-V_{1:k}^{\prime},\widetilde{a}_{1:k}\right\rangle}\right|\right]\right]}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{L}}_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\underbrace{{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}^{2}}\left[\left|{\left(\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}}-\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}^{\prime}}\right)\left\langle V_{1:k}^{\prime},\widetilde{a}_{1:k}\right\rangle}\right|\right]\right]}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{L}}_{2}}.

(175)

This leaves us two terms to control.

Step 4: lower bounds on
\mathcal{L}_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}
.

Next, we would like to control the two terms on the right-hand side of (
175
) separately.
Before continuing,
we introduce some additional notation: for any
j=1,2,\ldots,k
, define

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}_{j}\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}(V_{j}-V_{j}^{\prime}),\qquad\check{V}\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}(\overline{V}-\overline{V}^{\prime}),\qquad\widetilde{V}_{1:k}\coloneqq\frac{1}{2}(V_{1:k}-V_{1:k}^{\prime}).

(176)

It is straightforward to see that since
V_{j},V_{j}^{\prime}\in\{\pm 1\}
,
we have
\widetilde{V}_{j}\in\{-1,0,1\}
for all
j=1,2,\ldots,k
.

•

With regards to
{\mathcal{L}}_{1}
, recognizing that
\left|{\epsilon\overline{V}}\right|\leq\left|{\epsilon}\right|\leq 1/4
, one has

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{1}\geq\frac{4\epsilon}{3}{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}^{2}}\left[\big|\big\langle\widetilde{V}_{1:k},\widetilde{a}_{1:k}\big\rangle\big|\right]\right],

(177)

the right-hand side of which is further controlled by the following lemma. The proof can be found in
Section

C.4.4
.

Lemma C.9
.

Let
k\geq 64/\alpha
and
\epsilon\leq\min\left\{\frac{\alpha^{5/2}}{200},\frac{1}{64}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha k}{n\log(2nk/\alpha^{2})}}\right\}
. For any admissible algorithm whose resulting prediction set
{\mathcal{C}}
is the union of at most
k
intervals, we have

{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}^{2}}\left[\big|\big\langle\widetilde{V}_{1:k},\widetilde{a}_{1:k}\big\rangle\big|\right]\right]\geq\left(\frac{\sigma_{\pi}^{2}}{2}-\frac{{\alpha}}{16}\right)^{\frac{5}{2}}\frac{2}{\sqrt{k}},

where we define

\displaystyle\sigma_{\pi}^{2}\coloneqq{{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\Bigg[\bigg(\mu({\mathcal{C}})\big(1-\mu({\mathcal{C}})\big)-\frac{2K}{k}\bigg)_{+}\Bigg]}.

(178)

Consequently, taking (
177
) and Lemma
C.9
collectively reveals that

{\mathcal{L}}_{1}\geq\frac{8\epsilon}{3\sqrt{k}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\pi}^{2}}{2}-\frac{{\alpha}}{16}\right)^{\frac{5}{2}}.

(179)

•

When it comes to
{\mathcal{L}}_{2}
, we make the following observation:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{2}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}^{2}}\left[\left|{\left(\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}}-\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}^{\prime}}\right)\left\langle V_{1:k}^{\prime},\widetilde{a}_{1:k}\right\rangle}\right|\right]\right]

(180)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(a)}}{\leq}{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}^{2}}\left[\left|{\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}}-\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}^{\prime}}}\right|\right]\right]\overset{\mathrm{(b)}}{\leq}4\epsilon^{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}^{2}}\left[\big|\overline{V}^{\prime}-\overline{V}\big|\right]\right]

\displaystyle=8\epsilon^{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\left[{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}^{2}}\left[\big|\check{V}\big|\right]\right]\overset{\mathrm{(c)}}{\leq}8\epsilon^{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\Bigg[\bigg({\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}_{Q}^{2}}\bigg[\frac{1}{k^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\widetilde{V}_{j}^{2}\bigg]\bigg)^{1/2}\Bigg]\overset{\mathrm{(d)}}{\leq}\frac{8\epsilon^{2}}{\sqrt{k}}.

Here, (a) is valid since

\left|{\left\langle V_{1:k}^{\prime},\widetilde{a}_{1:k}\right\rangle}\right|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left|{\widetilde{a}_{j}}\right|\leq\mu([0,1])=1
(see (
166
));
(b) follows from the fact that

\min\{1+\epsilon\overline{V},\,1+\epsilon\overline{V}^{\prime}\}\geq 1-\epsilon\geq 1/2
; (c) applies Jensen’s inequality and uses the properties that

\{\widetilde{V}_{j}\}
are independent zero-mean random variables;
and (d) arises from the inequality
\widetilde{V}_{j}^{2}\leq 1
.

Combine Eqns. (
179
) and (
180
) with (
175
) to yield

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\left[{\mathcal{L}}_{Q}(Y_{1:n},U)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}{\mathcal{L}}_{1}-\frac{1}{2}{\mathcal{L}}_{2}

(181)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{4\epsilon}{3\sqrt{k}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\pi}^{2}}{2}-\frac{{\alpha}}{16}\right)^{\frac{5}{2}}-\frac{4\epsilon^{2}}{\sqrt{k}}=\frac{4\epsilon}{3\sqrt{k}}\left(\left(\frac{\sigma_{\pi}^{2}}{2}-\frac{{\alpha}}{16}\right)^{\frac{5}{2}}-3\epsilon\right).

Step 5: putting all pieces together.

To finish up, we divide into two cases and analyze them separately.

•

If
{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|{\mu({\mathcal{C}})-(1-\alpha)}\right|>\alpha/8\right)\geq 1/4
, then one has

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{V_{1:n}}\bigg[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{{\mathcal{D}}^{n}\times p_{U}}\left[\left|{{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n+1}\in{\mathcal{C}}\mid Y_{1:n},U\big)-(1-\alpha)}\right|\right]\bigg]\geq{\mathbb{E}}\left[\big|\mu(C)-(1-\alpha)\big|-\left|{\frac{\epsilon}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}}\left\langle V_{1:k},\widetilde{a}_{1:k}\right\rangle}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{\alpha}{32}-2\epsilon{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\bigg|\,\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}V_{j}\widetilde{a}_{j}\,\bigg|\bigg]\geq\frac{\alpha}{32}-2\epsilon{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\sqrt{\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{k}V_{j}^{2}\Big)\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}\Big)}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\overset{\mathrm{(e)}}{\geq}\frac{\alpha}{32}-\epsilon\overset{\mathrm{(f)}}{\geq}\frac{\alpha}{32}-\frac{\alpha}{64}=\frac{\alpha}{64},

where (e) invokes
Lemma

C.7
, and (f) is due to our choice of
\epsilon
.

•

If instead
{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|{\mu({\mathcal{C}})-(1-\alpha)}\right|>\alpha/8\right)<1/4
, then it holds that

{\mathbb{P}}\left(1-\frac{9\alpha}{8}\leq\mu({\mathcal{C}})\leq 1-\frac{7\alpha}{8}\right)\geq\frac{3}{4}.

(182)

We can then lower bound
\sigma_{\pi}^{2}
(cf. (
178
)) by

\displaystyle\sigma_{\pi}^{2}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[\bigg(\mu({\mathcal{C}})\big(1-\mu({\mathcal{C}})\big)-\frac{2K}{k}\bigg)_{+}\right]

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{E}}\left[\bigg(\mu({\mathcal{C}})\big(1-\mu({\mathcal{C}})\big)-\frac{2K}{k}\bigg)_{+}\mathbbm{1}\left\{1-\frac{9\alpha}{8}\leq\mu({\mathcal{C}})\leq 1-\frac{7\alpha}{8}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\bigg(\Big(1-\frac{9\alpha}{8}\Big)\frac{7\alpha}{8}-\frac{2K}{k}\bigg)_{+}\mathbbm{1}\left\{1-\frac{9\alpha}{8}\leq\mu({\mathcal{C}})\leq 1-\frac{7\alpha}{8}\right\}\bigg]

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(g)}}{\geq}\Big(\frac{49\alpha}{128}-\frac{2K}{k}\Big)_{+}{\mathbb{P}}\left(1-\frac{9\alpha}{8}\leq\mu({\mathcal{C}})\leq 1-\frac{7\alpha}{8}\right)\geq\frac{3\alpha}{8}\times\frac{3}{4}\geq\frac{9\alpha}{32}.

Here, (g) holds when
\alpha\leq 1/2
, and the last inequality follows by combining
k\geq\frac{256K}{\alpha}
and (
182
).
Taking this together with (
181
) and
\epsilon\leq\frac{\alpha^{5/2}}{200}
, we arrive at

{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\left[{\mathcal{L}}_{Q}(Y_{1:n},U)\right]\geq\frac{4\epsilon}{3\sqrt{k}}\left(\left(\frac{{9\alpha}}{32}-\frac{{\alpha}}{32}\right)^{\frac{5}{2}}-\frac{\alpha^{\frac{5}{2}}}{64}\right)\geq\frac{\alpha^{\frac{5}{2}}\epsilon}{48\sqrt{k}}.

(183)

Substituting Eqn. (
183
) into (
172
), we obtain

\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{V_{1:n}}\left[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{{\mathcal{D}}^{n}\times p_{U}}\Big[\big|{\mathbb{P}}(Y_{n+1}\in{\mathcal{C}}\mid Y_{1:n},U)-(1-\alpha)\big|\right]\Big]\geq\frac{1}{3}\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{Y_{1:n},U}[{\mathcal{L}}_{Q}(Y_{1:n},U)]-\frac{\alpha^{6}}{4n^{3}k^{3}}\geq\frac{\alpha^{\frac{5}{2}}\epsilon}{144\sqrt{k}}-\frac{\alpha^{6}}{4n^{3}k^{3}}.

The above two cases taken collectively conclude the proof of Lemma
C.6
.

C.4.2
Proof of
Lemma

C.7

Upper bound.

According to the definition of
a_{j}
and
\overline{a}
(see (
166
)), it is readily seen that

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\big(a_{j}-\overline{a}\big)^{2}=\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}a_{j}^{2}-k\overline{a}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{k}\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}a_{j}-\frac{\mu({\mathcal{C}})^{2}}{k}=\frac{\mu({\mathcal{C}})\big(1-\mu({\mathcal{C}})\big)}{k}\leq\frac{1}{4k},

where the first inequality follows since
a_{j}\leq\mu(I_{j})=1/k
and
\overline{a}=\mu(\mathcal{C})/k
, and the last inequality comes from the AM-GM inequality.

Lower bound.

To establish the lower bound, we exploit the structural property of the prediction set
{\mathcal{C}}
(i.e.,
{\mathcal{C}}
is the union of at most
K
intervals).
Consider the following conditions:

•

suppose
l
intervals from
\{I_{j}\}_{j=1}^{k}
(without loss of generality,
\{I_{j}\}_{j=1}^{l}
) are completely contained in
{\mathcal{C}}
;

•

at most
2K
of the
\{I_{j}\}_{j=1}^{k}
(without loss of generality,
\{I_{j_{i}}\}_{i=1}^{2K}
) are only partially covered by
{\mathcal{C}}
, which always holds due to the structural property of
{\mathcal{C}}
.

Since the intervals
\{I_{j}\}_{j=1}^{k}
are mutually disjoint, the total Lebesgue measure of these at most
l+2K
intervals must be at least
\mu({\mathcal{C}})
. This leads to

\frac{l+2K}{k}=\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{l}\big|\,I_{j}\,\big|+\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{2K}\big|\,I_{j_{i}}\,\big|\geq\mu({\mathcal{C}}),

thus indicating that

l\geq\big(\mu({\mathcal{C}})k-2K\big)_{+}.

(184)

Now, let us look at
\{\widetilde{a}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{k}
.
For each
1\leq j\leq l
, we have
a_{j}=\mu({\mathcal{C}}\cap I_{j})=1/k
and
k\overline{a}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}a_{j}=\mu({\mathcal{C}})
, allowing one to derive

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\big(a_{j}-\overline{a}\big)^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}a_{j}^{2}-k\overline{a}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}a_{j}^{2}-\frac{\mu({\mathcal{C}})^{2}}{k}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{j=1}^{l}a_{j}^{2}-\frac{\mu({\mathcal{C}})^{2}}{k}=\frac{l}{k^{2}}-\frac{\mu({\mathcal{C}})^{2}}{k}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:l_ge_muC-2K}}{\geq}\frac{1}{k}\bigg\{\Big(\mu({\mathcal{C}})-\frac{2K}{k}\Big)_{+}-\mu({\mathcal{C}})^{2}\bigg\}\geq\frac{1}{k}\bigg\{\mu({\mathcal{C}})\big(1-\mu({\mathcal{C}})\big)-\frac{2K}{k}\bigg\}.

This combined with the trivial fact
\sum_{j=1}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}\geq 0
establishes the advertised lower bound on
\sum_{j=1}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}
.

C.4.3
Proof of Lemma
C.8

By virtue of Bayes’s rule, the conditional density
p(V_{1:k}\mid y_{1:n},u)
admits the following expression:

\displaystyle p(V_{1:k}\mid y_{1:n},u)

\displaystyle=\frac{p(V_{1:k},y_{1:n},u)}{\sum_{v_{1:k}}p(v_{1:k},y_{1:n},u)}=\frac{\left(p_{U}(u)\prod_{j=1}^{k}(1+\epsilon V_{j})^{N_{j}}\right)\big/2^{k}(1+\epsilon\overline{V})^{n}}{\sum_{v_{1:k}}\left(p_{U}(u)\prod_{j=1}^{k}(1+\epsilon v_{j})^{N_{j}}\right)\big/2^{k}(1+\epsilon\overline{v})^{n}}

(185)

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k}(1+\epsilon V_{j})^{N_{j}}\right)\big/(1+\epsilon\overline{V})^{n}}{\sum_{v_{1:k}}\left[\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k}(1+\epsilon v_{j})^{N_{j}}\right)\big/(1+\epsilon\overline{v})^{n}\right]}.

For any given
V_{1:k}
, we observe that

\displaystyle\bigg(\prod\limits_{j=1}^{k}(1+\epsilon V_{j})^{N_{j}}\bigg)\Big/\left(1+\epsilon\overline{V}\right)^{n}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\prod\limits_{j=1}^{k}(1+\epsilon V_{j})^{N_{j}}\bigg)e^{-\epsilon\overline{V}n}\cdot\frac{e^{\epsilon\overline{V}n}}{\big(1+\epsilon\overline{V}\big)^{n}}

(186)

\displaystyle\geq\prod\limits_{j=1}^{k}(1+\epsilon V_{j})^{N_{j}}e^{-\epsilon V_{j}\frac{n}{k}}\eqqcolon\prod\limits_{j=1}^{k}q_{j}(V_{j},y_{1:n}),

where the last line follows from the elementary inequality
(e^{x})^{n}\geq(1+x)^{n}
for all
x\in[-1,1]
, and we define

q_{j}(V_{j},y_{1:n})\coloneqq(1+\epsilon V_{j})^{N_{j}}e^{-\epsilon V_{j}\frac{n}{k}}.

Further, define

\displaystyle q_{j}(y_{1:n})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{V_{j}}(1+\epsilon V_{j})^{N_{j}}e^{-\epsilon V_{j}\frac{n}{k}},\quad

\displaystyle j=1,\ldots,k;

(187a)

\displaystyle q(y_{1:n})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{V_{1:k}}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{k}\left[(1+\epsilon V_{j})^{N_{j}}e^{-\epsilon V_{j}\frac{n}{k}}\right]=\prod\limits_{j=1}^{k}q_{j}(y_{1:n});

(187b)

\displaystyle q_{j}(V_{j}\mid y_{1:n})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{q_{j}(V_{j},y_{1:n})}{q_{j}(y_{1:n})},\qquad

\displaystyle j=1,\ldots,k

(187c)

\displaystyle q(V_{1:k}\mid y_{1:n})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\prod_{j=1}^{k}q_{j}(V_{j}\,|\,y_{1:n}).

(187d)

Combining the above analysis and notation with a little algebra, we reach

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{V_{1:k}}

\displaystyle\bigg[{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}^{n}\times p_{U}}\Big[\big|\,{\mathbb{P}}(Y_{n+1}\in{\mathcal{C}}\,|\,Y_{1:n},U)-(1-\alpha)\,\big|\Big]\bigg]

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:cvg_err_to_lVC}}{=}\int\limits_{[0,1]^{n}\times{\mathcal{U}}}\bigg\{\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}p(V_{1:k}\mid y_{1:n},u)l(V_{1:k},{\mathcal{C}})\bigg\}p(y_{1:n},u){\textnormal{d}}y_{1:n}{\textnormal{d}}u

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:factor_approx_p1}}{=}\int\limits_{[0,1]^{n}\times{\mathcal{U}}}\bigg\{\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}\frac{\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{k}(1+\epsilon V_{j})^{N_{j}}\Big)\big(1+\epsilon\overline{V}\big)^{-n}}{\sum_{v_{1:k}}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{k}(1+\epsilon v_{j})^{N_{j}}\Big)(1+\overline{v})^{-n}}l(V_{1:k},{\mathcal{C}})\bigg\}p(y_{1:n},u){\textnormal{d}}y_{1:n}{\textnormal{d}}u

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:factor_approx_p2}}{\geq}\int\limits_{[0,1]^{n}\times{\mathcal{U}}}\left\{\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{k}q_{j}(V_{j},y_{1:n})}{\sum_{v_{1:k}}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{k}(1+\epsilon v_{j})^{N_{j}}\Big)(1+\epsilon\overline{v})^{-n}}l(V_{1:k},{\mathcal{C}})\right\}p(y_{1:n},u){\textnormal{d}}y_{1:n}{\textnormal{d}}u

(188)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:def_q_margin_cond}}{=}\int\limits_{[0,1]^{n}\times{\mathcal{U}}}\underbrace{\left(\frac{q(y_{1:n})}{\sum_{v_{1:k}}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{k}(1{+}\epsilon v_{j})^{N_{j}}\Big)(1{+}\epsilon\overline{v})^{-n}}\right)}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{G}}(y_{1:n})}\left\{\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{k}q_{j}(V_{j}\mid y_{1:n})l(V_{1:k},{\mathcal{C}})\right\}p(y_{1:n},u){\textnormal{d}}y_{1:n}{\textnormal{d}}u.

The next step is to control
{\mathcal{G}}(y_{1:n})
. Specifically, by expanding
q(y_{1:n})
and applying Eqn. (
185
), we can establish the following inequality:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{G}}(y_{1:n})

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:def_q_margin}}{=}\frac{\sum_{V_{1:k}}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{k}(1+\epsilon V_{j})^{N_{j}}\Big)\big(1+\epsilon\overline{V}\big)^{-n}\frac{(1+\epsilon\overline{V})^{n}}{e^{\epsilon\overline{V}n}}}{\sum_{v_{1:k}}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{k}(1+\epsilon v_{j})^{N_{j}}\Big)\big(1+\epsilon\overline{v}\big)^{-n}}\overset{\eqref{eq:factor_approx_p1}}{=}\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}p(V_{1:k}\mid y_{1:n})\frac{\big(1+\epsilon\overline{V}\big)^{n}}{e^{\epsilon\overline{V}n}}

(189)

\displaystyle\geq\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}p(V_{1:k}\mid y_{1:n})\frac{\big(1+\epsilon\overline{V}\big)^{n}}{e^{\epsilon\overline{V}n}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\left|{\overline{V}}\right|\leq\sqrt{6k^{-1}\log(2nk/\alpha^{2})}\right\}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(a)}}{\geq}\frac{1}{3}\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}p(V_{1:k}\mid y_{1:n})\mathbbm{1}\left\{\left|{\overline{V}}\right|\leq\sqrt{6k^{-1}\log(2nk/\alpha^{2})}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\left(1-{\mathbb{P}}\left(\overline{V}>\sqrt{\frac{6\log(2nk/\alpha^{2})}{k}}~\Bigg|~Y_{1:n}=y_{1:n}\right)\right).

To justify why (a) holds, note that

\epsilon\left|{\overline{V}}\right|\leq\sqrt{\frac{k}{12n\log(2nk/\alpha^{2})}}\sqrt{\frac{6\log(2nk/\alpha^{2})}{k}}=\sqrt{\frac{1}{2n}}

holds under the condition that
\left|{\overline{V}}\right|\leq\sqrt{2k^{-1}\log(nk/\alpha)}
,
which combined with the elementary fact
1+x+x^{2}\geq e^{x}

(x\in[-1,1])
gives

\frac{e^{\epsilon\overline{V}n}}{\big(1+\epsilon\overline{V}\big)^{n}}\leq\left(\frac{1+\epsilon\overline{V}+(\epsilon\overline{V})^{2}}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}}\right)^{n}\leq\left(1+2(\epsilon\overline{V})^{2}\right)^{n}\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\right)^{n}\leq e<3.

As a consequence, substituting Eqn. (
189
) into Eqn. (
188
) yields

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{V_{1:k}}\bigg[{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{D}}^{n}\times p_{U}}\Big[\left|{{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n+1}\in{\mathcal{C}}\,|\,Y_{1:n},U\big)-(1-\alpha)}\right|\Big]\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad\geq\frac{1}{3}\int\limits_{[0,1]^{n}\times{\mathcal{U}}}\left(1-{\mathbb{P}}\left(\overline{V}>\sqrt{\frac{6\log(2nk/\alpha^{2})}{k}}~\Bigg|~y_{1:n}\right)\right)\left\{\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{k}q_{j}(V_{j}\mid y_{1:n})l(V_{1:k},{\mathcal{C}})\right\}p(y_{1:n},u){\textnormal{d}}y_{1:n}{\textnormal{d}}u

\displaystyle\quad\overset{\mathrm{(b)}}{\geq}\frac{1}{3}\int\limits_{[0,1]^{n}\times{\mathcal{U}}}\left\{\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{k}q_{j}(V_{j}\mid y_{1:n})l(V_{1:k},{\mathcal{C}})\right\}p(y_{1:n},u){\textnormal{d}}y_{1:n}{\textnormal{d}}u

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 160.00024pt-\frac{1}{3}\int\limits_{[0,1]^{n}\times{\mathcal{U}}}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|{\overline{V}}\right|>\sqrt{\frac{6\log(2nk/\alpha^{2})}{k}}\Big|~y_{1:n}\right)p(y_{1:n},u){\textnormal{d}}y_{1:n}{\textnormal{d}}u

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{3}\int\limits_{[0,1]^{n}\times{\mathcal{U}}}{\mathcal{L}}_{Q}(y_{1:n})p(y_{1:n},u){\textnormal{d}}y_{1:n}{\textnormal{d}}u-\frac{1}{3}{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|{\overline{V}}\right|>\sqrt{\frac{6\log(2nk/\alpha^{2})}{k}}\right),

(190)

where (b) relies on the fact that

\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{k}q_{j}(V_{j}\mid y_{1:n})l(V_{1:k},{\mathcal{C}})\overset{\eqref{eq:def_l_VC}}{=}\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}\limits_{V_{1:k}\sim q(V_{1:k}\,|\,y_{1:n})}\Big[\Big|\,{\mathbb{P}}\big(Y_{n+1}\in{\mathcal{C}}\,|\,Y_{1:n},U\big)-(1-\alpha)\,\Big|\Big]\leq 1.

Finally, recalling that
V_{1:k}
are
k
independent Rademacher random variables, we can apply Hoeffding’s inequality to yield

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|{\overline{V}}\right|>\sqrt{\frac{6\log(2nk/\alpha^{2})}{k}}\right)\leq 2\exp\left\{-\frac{6k\log\frac{2nk}{\alpha^{2}}}{2k}\right\}\leq\frac{\alpha^{6}}{4n^{3}k^{3}}.

Substitution into the above bound (
190
) concludes the proof of Lemma
C.8
.

C.4.4
Proof of Lemma
C.9

As discussed earlier, we have
\widetilde{V}_{j}\in\{-1,0,1\}
(see (
176
)).
Further, define

\delta_{j}\coloneqq\mathbbm{1}\{\widetilde{V}_{j}\neq 0\},\qquad\text{and}\qquad\xi_{j}\coloneqq\begin{cases}\mathrm{sgn}\big(\widetilde{V}_{j}\big),&\text{if }\delta_{j}=1,\\[4.0pt]
\zeta_{j},&\text{if }\delta_{j}=0,\end{cases}

where
\{\zeta_{j}\}_{j=1}^{k}
are
k
independent Rademacher random variables, generated
independent of
(\delta_{j})_{j=1}^{k}
,
Y_{1:n}
and
U
.
It is then straightforward to verify that

\widetilde{V}_{j}=\xi_{j}\delta_{j}.

Additionally, it is easy to see that for any given
y_{1:n}
and
u
, one has

{\mathbb{P}}(\xi_{j}=1,\delta_{j}=1\mid y_{1:n},u)={\mathbb{P}}(V_{j}>V_{j}^{\prime}\mid y_{1:n},u)={\mathbb{P}}(V_{j}<V_{j}^{\prime}\mid y_{1:n},u)={\mathbb{P}}(\xi_{j}=-1,\delta_{j}=1\mid y_{1:n},u),

where the second identity follows from the fact that
V_{j}
and
V_{j}^{\prime}
are independently and identically distributed.
This implies that, conditional on
\delta_{j}=1
and
(y_{1:n},u)
, the variable
\xi_{j}
is a Rademacher random variable.
Similarly, when
\delta_{j}=0
and when
(y_{1:n},u)
are given, we have
\xi_{j}=\zeta_{j}
, which is also Rademacher.

Moreover, since
\{V_{j}\}_{j=1}^{k}
and
\{V_{j}^{\prime}\}_{j=1}^{k}
are independent within each sequence
(with
V_{1:k}\sim\prod_{j=1}^{k}q_{j}(V_{j}\mid y_{1:n},u)
),
it follows that
\{\xi_{j}\}_{j=1}^{k}
are independent Rademacher random variables, which are also
independent of
\big(\{\delta_{j}\}_{j=1}^{k},Y_{1:n},U\big)
. Therefore, for any fixed
\big(\{\delta_{j}\}_{j=1}^{k},y_{1:n},u\big)
, applying Lemma
E.2
to
\{\xi_{j}\}_{j=1}^{k}
yields

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\big|\big\langle\widetilde{V}_{1:k},\widetilde{a}_{1:k}\big\rangle\big|\Big]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\bigg|\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\xi_{j}\delta_{j}\widetilde{a}_{j}\bigg|\Bigg]={\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\bigg|\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\xi_{j}\delta_{j}\widetilde{a}_{j}\bigg|~\Big|~\{\delta_{j}\}_{j=1}^{k},~\{\widetilde{a}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{k}\Bigg]\Bigg]

(191)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\bigg(\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}\delta_{j}^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg]=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\bigg(\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}\delta_{j}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Bigg],

where the last equality holds since
\delta_{j}^{2}=\delta_{j}
.

To continue, let us first examine

{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}\delta_{j}\right].

Note that, given
(y_{1:n},u)
, under the distribution

\prod_{j=1}^{k}q_{j}(V_{j}\mid y_{1:n},u)
one has

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\delta_{j}\mid y_{1:n},u]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}(V_{j}\neq V_{j}^{\prime}\mid y_{1:n},u)=2q_{j}(1\mid y_{1:n},u)q_{j}(0\mid y_{1:n},u)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(1-(2q_{j}(1\mid y_{1:n},u)-1)^{2}\right)\eqqcolon\frac{\left(1-(2q_{j}-1)^{2}\right)}{2},

where we define

q_{j}\coloneqq q_{j}(1\mid y_{1:n},u)=\frac{(1+\epsilon)^{N_{j}}e^{-\epsilon\frac{n}{k}}}{\sum_{V=\pm 1}(1+\epsilon V)^{N_{j}}e^{-\epsilon\frac{n}{k}V}}.

(192)

From this, we can derive that

{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}\delta_{j}\Bigg]=\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}\Bigg]-\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}(2q_{j}-1)^{2}\Bigg].

(193)

As for the first term on the right-hand side of (
193
), applying
Lemma

C.7
and then taking expectation over
Y_{1:n}
and
U
yield

\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sum_{j=1}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}\bigg]\geq\frac{1}{2k}{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\Big(\mu({\mathcal{C}})\big(1-\mu({\mathcal{C}})\big)-\frac{2K}{k}\Big)_{+}\bigg]

(194)

Also, the second term on the right-hand side of (
193
) satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}(2q_{j}-1)^{2}\Bigg]\leq\frac{1}{2k^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}(2q_{j}-1)^{2}\right].

(195)

To control
{\mathbb{E}}\big[\sum_{j=1}^{k}(2q_{j}-1)^{2}\big]
, we resort to the following lemma, with the proof given in
Section

C.4.5
.

Lemma C.10
.

Recall that
q_{j}
is defined in (
192
). For any admissible algorithm whose resulting prediction set
{\mathcal{C}}
is the union of at most
k
intervals, we have

{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}(2q_{j}-1)^{2}\Bigg]\leq 321n\epsilon^{2}\log\Big(\frac{2nk}{\alpha^{2}}\Big)+\frac{\alpha^{6}}{32n^{3}k^{3}}.

Taking Eqns. (
193
), (
194
), (
195
) and Lemma
C.10
together yields

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}\delta_{j}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2k}{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\Big(\mu({\mathcal{C}})\big(1-\mu({\mathcal{C}})\big)-\frac{2K}{k}\Big)_{+}\bigg]-\frac{1}{k^{2}}\left(161n\epsilon^{2}\log\Big(\frac{2nk}{\alpha^{2}}\Big)+\frac{\alpha^{6}}{64n^{3}k^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sigma_{\pi}^{2}}{2k}-\frac{\alpha}{16k},

(196)

where the last line holds as long as
k\geq\frac{256K}{\alpha}
and
\epsilon\leq\min\left\{\frac{\alpha^{5/2}}{200},\frac{1}{64}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha k}{n\log(2nk/\alpha^{2})}}\right\}
.

To finish up, denoting
{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}\delta_{j}\right]
as
\lambda
, and applying Lemma
E.3
with
\theta=1/2
, we arrive at

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sqrt{\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}\delta_{j}}\,\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{\lambda}{\mathbb{P}}\Bigg(\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}\delta\geq\frac{\lambda}{2}\Bigg)\geq\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{\lambda}\frac{\lambda^{2}}{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\big(\sum_{j=1}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}\delta_{j}\big)^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}\frac{\lambda^{5/2}}{{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\big(\sum_{j=1}^{k}\widetilde{a}_{j}^{2}\big)^{2}\Big]}\overset{\text{Lemma }\ref{lem:range_of_sum_tilde_a_j2}}{\geq}\frac{k^{2}}{4\sqrt{2}}\frac{\lambda^{5/2}}{{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\mu({\mathcal{C}})^{2}\big(1-\mu({\mathcal{C}})\big)^{2}\Big]}

\displaystyle\geq 2\sqrt{2}k^{2}\lambda^{5/2}\overset{\eqref{eq:lower_bd_EB_sum_a2_delta}}{\geq}\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{k}}\left(\frac{\sigma_{\pi}^{2}}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{16}\right)^{\frac{5}{2}}.

Combining this with Eqn. (
191
) establishes the advertised result of the lemma.

C.4.5
Proof of Lemma
C.10

To begin with, the TV distance between two Bernoulli distributions
\mathrm{Ber}(q_{j})
and
\mathrm{Ber}(1/2)
obeys

\mathsf{TV}\big(\mathrm{Ber}(q_{j}),~\mathrm{Ber}(1/2)\big)=\left|{q_{j}-\frac{1}{2}}\right|+\left|{(1-q_{j})-\frac{1}{2}}\right|=\left|{2q_{j}-1}\right|,

which combined with Pinsker’s inequality
(
73
, Lemm 2.5)
yields

(2q_{j}-1)^{2}=\big(\mathsf{TV}\big(\mathrm{Ber}(q_{j}),~\mathrm{Ber}(1/2)\big)\big)^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{KL}\left(\mathrm{Ber}(q_{j})\,\big\|\,\mathrm{Ber}(0.5)\right).

(197)

Note that the distribution

q(V_{1:k}\mid y_{1:n})=\prod_{j=1}^{k}q_{j}(V_{j}\mid y_{1:n})

factorizes.
Therefore, recalling the definition of
q_{j}
in (
192
), one can apply the chain rule of the KL divergence to derive

\displaystyle 2{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}(2q_{j}-1)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n}}\Bigg[\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\mathsf{KL}\left(\mathrm{Ber}(q_{j})\,\big\|\,\mathrm{Ber}(0.5)\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n}}\Bigg[\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}\bigg(\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{j}\sim q_{j}(\cdot|Y_{1:n})}\Big[\log\big(2q_{j}(V_{j}\,|\,Y_{1:n})\big)\Big]\bigg)\Bigg]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n}}\Bigg[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim q(\cdot|Y_{1:n})}\bigg[\log\bigg(2^{k}\prod\limits_{j=1}^{k}q_{j}(V_{j}\,|\,Y_{1:n})\bigg)\bigg]\Bigg]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n}}\Bigg[\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}q(V_{1:k}\,|\,Y_{1:n})\log\big(2^{k}q(V_{1:k}\,|\,Y_{1:n})\big)\Bigg].

Recognizing that
q(V_{1:k}\,|\,Y_{1:n})
is an approximation of
p(V_{1:k}\,|\,Y_{1:n},U)
(recall the arguments in (
185
)-(
187
)), we consider the following decomposition by incorporating the term
p(V_{1:k}\,|\,Y_{1:n},U)
into the preceding identity:

\displaystyle 2{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}(2q_{j}-1)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\Bigg[\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}p(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n},U)\frac{q(V_{1:k}\,|\,Y_{1:n})}{p(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n},U)}\log\bigg(2^{k}q(V_{1:k}\,|\,Y_{1:n})\bigg)\Bigg]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\left[\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}\frac{q(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n})}{p(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n},U)}\log\big(2^{k}p(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n},U)\big)p(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n},U)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\left[\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}\frac{q(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n})}{p(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n},U)}\log\bigg(\frac{q(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n})}{p(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg)p(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n},U)\right].

(198)

Next, we examine the ratio term
\frac{q(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n})}{p(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n},U)}
, which appears multiple times in (
198
). From the elementary fact
1+x\leq e^{x}
as well as the definitions (
185
) and (
187
), we can demonstrate that

\displaystyle\frac{q(V_{1:k}\mid y_{1:n})}{p(V_{1:k}\mid y_{1:n},u)}=\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{k}\Big((1+\epsilon V_{j})^{N_{j}}e^{-\epsilon V_{j}\frac{n}{k}}\Big)}{\sum_{v_{1:k}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\Big((1+\epsilon v_{j})^{N_{j}}e^{-\epsilon v_{j}\frac{n}{k}}\Big)}\cdot\frac{\sum_{v_{1:k}}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{k}(1+\epsilon v_{j})^{N_{j}}\Big)(1+\epsilon\overline{v})^{-n}}{\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{k}(1+\epsilon V_{j})^{N_{j}}\Big)(1+\epsilon\overline{V})^{-n}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt=\frac{(1+\epsilon\overline{V})^{n}}{e^{\epsilon\overline{V}n}}\cdot\frac{\sum_{v_{1:k}}\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{k}(1+\epsilon v_{j})^{N_{j}}\Big)(1+\epsilon\overline{v})^{-n}}{\sum_{v_{1:k}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\Big((1+\epsilon v_{j})^{N_{j}}e^{-\epsilon v_{j}\frac{n}{k}}\Big)}=\frac{(1+\epsilon\overline{V})^{n}}{e^{\epsilon\overline{V}n}}\bigg/\left(\sum\limits_{v_{1:k}}p(v_{1:k}\mid y_{1:n},u)\frac{(1+\epsilon\overline{v})^{n}}{e^{\epsilon\overline{v}n}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt=\frac{F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})}{{\mathbb{E}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|y_{1:n},u)}\big[F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})\big]}=\frac{F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})}{F(y_{1:n},u)}\leq\frac{1}{F(y_{1:n},u)},

(199)

where we define

F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})\coloneqq\frac{(1+\epsilon\overline{V})^{n}}{e^{\epsilon\overline{V}n}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad F(y_{1:n},u)\coloneqq{\mathbb{E}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|y_{1:n},u)}\big[F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})\big].

(200)

Further, given that the choice of
\epsilon
guarantees that
\left|{\epsilon\overline{V}}\right|<1/2
, we can invoke the elementary inequality
1+x\leq e^{x}\leq 1+x+x^{2}
(
|x|<1/2
) to derive

1\leq\frac{1}{F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})}=\frac{e^{\epsilon\overline{V}n}}{\big(1+\epsilon\overline{V}\big)^{n}}\leq\left(\frac{1+\epsilon\overline{V}+\epsilon^{2}\overline{V}^{2}}{1+\epsilon\overline{V}}\right)^{n}\leq\left(1+2\epsilon^{2}\overline{V}^{2}\right)^{n}\leq\exp\left\{2\big(\epsilon\overline{V}\big)^{2}n\right\}.

(201)

Substituting (
199
) and (
201
) into (
198
) leads to

\displaystyle 2{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}(2q_{j}-1)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\left[\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}\frac{F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})}{F(Y_{1:n},U)}\log\big(2^{k}p(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n},U)\big)p(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n},U)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\left[\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}\frac{F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})}{F(Y_{1:n},U)}\log\bigg(\frac{F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})}{F(Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg)p(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n},U)\right]

\displaystyle=\underset{\eqqcolon\,\mathcal{S}_{0}}{\underbrace{{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\bigg[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\Big[\log\big(2^{k}p(V_{1:k}\,|\,Y_{1:n},U)\big)\Big]\bigg]}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\left[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\left[\bigg(\frac{F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})}{F(Y_{1:n},U)}-1\bigg)\log\big(2^{k}p(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n},U)\big)\right]\right]}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{S}}_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\Bigg[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg[\frac{F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})}{F(Y_{1:n},U)}\log\bigg(\frac{F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})}{F(Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg)\bigg]\Bigg]}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{S}}_{2}},

(202)

leaving us with three terms to cope with.

Bounding the term
\mathcal{S}_{0}
.

Towards this end, we first define

I(V_{1:k};Y_{1:n})
to be the mutual information between
V_{1:k}
and
Y_{1:n}
, namely,

I(V_{1:k};Y_{1:n})\coloneqq\sum_{V_{1:k}}\int_{[0,1]^{n}}\log\!\left(\frac{p(V_{1:k},y_{1:n})}{2^{-k}p(y_{1:n})}\right)\,p(V_{1:k},y_{1:n})\,{\textnormal{d}}y_{1:n},

(203)

where

p(V_{1:k},y_{1:n})\coloneqq\int_{{\mathcal{U}}}p(V_{1:k},y_{1:n},u)\,{\textnormal{d}}u\qquad\text{and}\qquad p(y_{1:n})\coloneqq\sum_{V_{1:k}}p(V_{1:k},y_{1:n}).

(204)

As it turns out, the term
\mathcal{S}_{0}
(cf. (
202
)) is equivalent to this mutual information quantity since

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{0}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\bigg[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\Big[\log\big(2^{k}p(V_{1:k}\,|\,Y_{1:n},U)\big)\Big]\bigg]

\displaystyle=\sum_{V_{1:k}}\int_{[0,1]^{n}\times{\mathcal{U}}}p(V_{1:k},y_{1:n})p_{U}(u)\log\Big(\frac{p(V_{1:k},y_{1:n})p_{U}(u)}{2^{-k}p(y_{1:n})p_{U}(u)}\Big){\textnormal{d}}y_{1:n}{\textnormal{d}}u

\displaystyle=\sum_{V_{1:k}}\int_{[0,1]^{n}}p(V_{1:k},y_{1:n})\log\Big(\frac{p(V_{1:k},y_{1:n})}{2^{-k}p(y_{1:n})}\Big){\textnormal{d}}y_{1:n}=I(V_{1:k};Y_{1:n}),

where the second line holds since
U
is independent of
(V_{1:k},Y_{1:n})
(as it only affects the construction of the prediction set
{\mathcal{C}}
).

According to the data-generating mechanism and the chain rule of the KL divergence, one has

\displaystyle I(V_{1:k},Y_{1:n})

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}\int_{[0,1]^{n}}p(V_{1:k})p(y_{1:n}\mid V_{1:k})\log\frac{p(V_{1:k})p(y_{1:n}\mid V_{1:k})}{p(V_{1:k})p(y_{1:n})}{\textnormal{d}}y_{1:n}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{n}\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}\int_{[0,1]}\frac{p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})}{2^{k}}\log\frac{p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})}{p(y_{i})}{\textnormal{d}}y_{i}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(a)}}{\leq}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{n}\sum_{V_{1:k}}\frac{1}{2^{k}}\int_{[0,1]}\left(p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})-p(y_{i})+p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})\left(\frac{p(y_{i})}{p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})}-1\right)^{2}\right){\textnormal{d}}y_{i}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(b)}}{=}\sum\limits_{{i}={1}}^{n}\sum\limits_{V_{1:k}}\int_{[0,1]}\frac{p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})}{2^{k}}\left(\frac{p(y_{i})}{p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})}-1\right)^{2}{\textnormal{d}}y_{i}\overset{\mathrm{(c)}}{\leq}36n\epsilon^{2},

(205)

where (b) follows because both
p(y_{i})
and
p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})
are density functions;
(a) and (c) are proven below.

•

To justify (c), suppose that
y_{i}\in I_{j}
(defined in (
154
)). Denoting by
V_{1:k}^{\prime}
an independent copy of
V_{1:k}
, one can derive

\displaystyle\left(\frac{p(y_{i})}{p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})}-1\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{{\mathbb{E}}_{V_{1:k}^{\prime}}[p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k}^{\prime})]}{p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})}-1\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{V_{1:k}^{\prime}}\left[\left(\frac{p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k}^{\prime})}{p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})}-1\right)^{2}\right]={\mathbb{E}}_{V_{1:k}^{\prime}}\left[\left(\frac{(1+\epsilon V_{j}^{\prime})(1+\epsilon\overline{V})}{(1+\epsilon V_{j})(1+\epsilon\overline{V}^{\prime})}-1\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{V_{1:k}^{\prime}}\left[\left(\frac{\epsilon(V_{j}^{\prime}+\overline{V}-V_{j}-\overline{V}^{\prime})+\epsilon^{2}(V_{j}^{\prime}\overline{V}-V_{j}\overline{V}^{\prime})}{(1+\epsilon V_{j})(1+\epsilon\overline{V}^{\prime})}\right)^{2}\right]\leq 36\epsilon^{2},

with the proviso that
\epsilon\leq 1/6
. This also implies that

\displaystyle\left|{\frac{p(y_{i})}{p(y_{i}\,|\,V_{1:k})}-1}\right|\leq 1\qquad\text{as long as }\epsilon\leq\frac{1}{6}.

(206)

•

Regarding inequality (a), we invoke the elementary fact
\log(1+x)\geq x-x^{2}
for
x\in[-1,1]
along with (
206
) to reach

\displaystyle p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})\log\frac{p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})}{p(y_{i})}

\displaystyle=-p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})\log\frac{p(y_{i})}{p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})}

\displaystyle\leq-p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})\left(\frac{p(y_{i})}{p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})}-1-\left(\frac{p(y_{i})}{p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})}-1\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})-p(y_{i})+p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})\left(\frac{p(y_{i})}{p(y_{i}\mid V_{1:k})}-1\right)^{2}.

Bounding the term
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
.

Based on the definition (cf. (
202
)), we can write

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{1}={\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\bigg[\underbrace{\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg[\Big(F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})-F(Y_{1:n},U)\Big)\log\big(2^{k}p(V_{1:k}\,|\,Y_{1:n},U)\big)\bigg]}_{\eqqcolon\,{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{\mathsf{num}}(Y_{1:n},U)}\Big/F(Y_{1:n},U)\bigg].

(207)

We begin by examining
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{\mathsf{num}}(Y_{1:n},U)
.
For notational simplicity, set

\sigma\coloneqq\sqrt{16k^{-1}\log(2nk/\alpha^{2})}.

(208)

Consider any given
y_{1:n},u
, and any two realizations
v_{1:k},~v_{1:k}^{\prime}
. If
|\overline{v}|\vee|\overline{v}^{\prime}|\leq\sigma
, then by the mean value theorem, there exists a real number
\zeta
between
\epsilon\overline{v}
and
\epsilon\overline{v}^{\prime}
(which means
|\zeta|\leq\epsilon\big(|\overline{v}|\vee|\overline{v}^{\prime}|\big)\leq\epsilon\sigma
) satisfying

\displaystyle\Big|\,F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{v})-F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{v}^{\prime})\,\Big|\leq\big|\,F_{n}^{\prime}(\zeta)\,\big|\cdot\big|\,\epsilon\overline{v}-\epsilon\overline{v}^{\prime}\,\big|\leq 2\epsilon n\sigma|\zeta|e^{-\zeta}\Big(\frac{1+\zeta}{e^{\zeta}}\Big)^{n-1}\leq 6\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}n,

(209)

where the choice of
\epsilon
ensures
\epsilon\sigma<1

and we have used the definition (
200
) of
F_{n}(\cdot)
.
Splitting the expectation in the definition of
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{\mathsf{num}}(Y_{1:n},U)
into two parts
based on whether or not
|\overline{V}|\vee|\overline{V}^{\prime}|\leq\sigma
, we can derive

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{\mathsf{num}}(Y_{1:n},U)=\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg[\Big(F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})-\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V^{\prime}_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\big[F_{n}\big(\epsilon\overline{V}^{\prime}\big)\big]\Big)\log\big(2^{k}p(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n},U)\big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k},V^{\prime}_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg[\Big|F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})-F_{n}\big(\epsilon\overline{V}^{\prime}\big)\Big|\log\Big(\frac{1}{p(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n},U)}\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\overset{\eqref{eq:lagrangian_mean_of_F_n}}{\leq}6\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}n\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg[\log\frac{1}{p(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\hskip 20.00003pt+\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k},V^{\prime}_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg[\Big|F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})-F_{n}\big(\epsilon\overline{V}^{\prime}\big)\Big|\mathbbm{1}\{|\overline{V}|\vee|\overline{V}^{\prime}|>\sigma\}\log\Big(\frac{1}{p(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n},U)}\Big)\bigg],

(210)

where the second line follows from Jensen’s inequality and the following:

\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k},V^{\prime}_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg[\Big(F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})-F_{n}\big(\epsilon\overline{V}^{\prime}\big)\Big)\log\big(2^{k}\big)\bigg]=0.

•

Regarding the first term on the right-hand side of (
210
), it is observed that for any given
y_{1:n}
and
u
,

\displaystyle\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|y_{1:n},u)}\bigg[\log\frac{1}{p(V_{1:k}\mid y_{1:n},u)}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\log 2^{k}-\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|y_{1:n},u)}\big[\log\big(2^{k}p(V_{1:k}\mid y_{1:n},u)\big)\big]

\displaystyle=k\log 2-\mathsf{KL}\big(p(V_{1:k}\mid y_{1:n},u)\,\|\,p(V_{1:k})\big)\leq k\log 2.

Plug this into the first term on the right-hand side of (
210
) and use (
208
) to yield

\displaystyle 6\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}n\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg[\log\frac{1}{p(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg]\leq 96\epsilon^{2}n\log\frac{2nk}{\alpha^{2}}.

(211)

•

As for the second term on the right-hand side of (
210
), it can be derived that

\displaystyle\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k},V^{\prime}_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg[\Big|F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})-F_{n}\big(\epsilon\overline{V}^{\prime}\big)\Big|\mathbbm{1}\{|\overline{V}|\vee|\overline{V}^{\prime}|>\sigma\}\log\Big(\frac{1}{p(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n},U)}\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:F_n_inverse_upper_bd}}{\leq}\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k},V^{\prime}_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg[\mathbbm{1}\Big\{\big|\overline{V}\big|\vee\big|\overline{V}^{\prime}\big|>\sigma\Big\}\log\Big(\frac{1}{p(V_{1:k}\mid Y_{1:n},U)}\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(a)}}{\leq}4\epsilon n{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\big|\overline{V}\big|\vee\big|\overline{V}^{\prime}\big|>\sigma\,\Big|\,Y_{1:n},U\bigg).

(212)

To justify step (a) of (
212
), consider any two different realization
v_{1:k}
and
v_{1:k}^{\prime}
, which obey

\frac{p(v_{1:k}^{\prime}\mid y_{1:n},u)}{p(v_{1:k}\mid y_{1:n},u)}=\frac{p(v_{1:k}^{\prime})p(y_{1:n}\mid v_{1:k}^{\prime})p_{U}(u)}{p(v_{1:k})p(y_{1:n}\mid v_{1:k})p_{U}(u)}=\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{k}(1+\epsilon v_{j}^{\prime})^{N_{j}}}{\prod_{j=1}^{k}(1+\epsilon v_{j})^{N_{j}}}\leq e^{4\epsilon n},

\Longrightarrow\qquad\log\Big(\frac{1}{p(V_{1:k}\mid y_{1:n},u)}\Big)=\log\bigg(\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}^{\prime}\mid y_{1:n},u}\Big[\frac{p(V_{1:k}^{\prime}\mid y_{1:n},u)}{p(V_{1:k}\mid y_{1:n},u)}\Big]\bigg)\leq 4\epsilon n.

Consequently, by substituting (
212
) and (
211
) into (
210
), we arrive at

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{\mathsf{num}}(Y_{1:n},U)

\displaystyle\leq 96\epsilon^{2}n\log\frac{2nk}{\alpha^{2}}+8\epsilon n{\mathbb{P}}\Big(|\overline{V}|>\sigma\,\Big|\,Y_{1:n},U\Big).

(213)

Now, we switch attention to the term
\frac{1}{F(Y_{1:n},U)}
.
Applying Jensen’s inequality yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\big[F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})\big]}\leq\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\left[\frac{e^{\epsilon\overline{V}n}}{\big(1+\epsilon\overline{V}\big)^{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\left[\frac{e^{\epsilon\overline{V}n}}{\big(1+\epsilon\overline{V}\big)^{n}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\left|{\overline{V}}\right|\leq\sigma\right\}\right]+\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\left[\frac{e^{\epsilon\overline{V}n}}{\big(1+\epsilon\overline{V}\big)^{n}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\left|{\overline{V}}\right|>\sigma\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\overset{\eqref{eq:F_n_inverse_upper_bd}}{\leq}\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\left[e^{2\big(\epsilon\overline{V}\big)^{2}n}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\left|{\overline{V}}\right|\leq\sigma\right\}\right]+\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\left[e^{2\big(\epsilon\overline{V}\big)^{2}n}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\left|{\overline{V}}\right|>\sigma\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 3+\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\left[\exp\left\{2\big(\epsilon\overline{V}\big)^{2}n\right\}\mathbbm{1}\left\{\left|{\overline{V}}\right|>\sigma\right\}\right],

(214)

where the last line follows since

2\big(\epsilon\overline{V}\big)^{2}n\leq\frac{2k}{32n}\cdot\frac{16n}{k}=1

provided that
\epsilon\leq\min\left\{\frac{\alpha^{5/2}}{128},\frac{1}{64}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha k}{n\log(2nk/\alpha^{2})}}\right\}
.

We are now ready to bound
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
. It is readily seen from (
207
) that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\left[\frac{{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{\mathsf{num}}(Y_{1:n},U)}{F(Y_{1:n},U)}\right]\overset{\eqref{eq:gS_1_numerator_fin}}{\leq}{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\bigg[\frac{96\epsilon^{2}n\log\Big(\frac{2nk}{\alpha^{2}}\Big)+8\epsilon n{\mathbb{P}}\Big(|\overline{V}|>\sigma\mid Y_{1:n},U\Big)}{F(Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg]

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:gS_1_denominator_bd}}{\leq}\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\Bigg[\bigg(96\epsilon^{2}n\log\Big(\frac{2nk}{\alpha^{2}}\Big)+8\epsilon n{\mathbb{P}}\Big(|\overline{V}|>\sigma\mid Y_{1:n},U\Big)\bigg)\bigg(3+\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\Big[e^{2(\epsilon\overline{V})^{2}n}\mathbbm{1}\{|\overline{V}|>\sigma\}\Big]\bigg)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq 100\epsilon^{2}n\log\Big(\frac{2nk}{\alpha^{2}}\Big)\bigg(3+{\mathbb{E}}_{V_{1:k}}\Big[e^{2(\epsilon\overline{V})^{2}n}\mathbbm{1}\{|\overline{V}|>\sigma\}\Big]\bigg)+24\epsilon n{\mathbb{P}}\big(|\overline{V}|>\sigma\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt+8\epsilon n\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\Bigg[{\mathbb{P}}\Big(|\overline{V}|>\sigma\mid Y_{1:n},U\Big)\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\Big[e^{2(\epsilon\overline{V})^{2}n}\mathbbm{1}\{|\overline{V}|>\sigma\}\Big]\Bigg].

(215)

Note that Hoeffding’s inequality tells us that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{V_{1:k}}\big(|\overline{V}|>\sigma\big)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}_{V_{1:k}}\bigg(|\overline{V}|>\sqrt{\frac{16\log(2nk/\alpha^{2})}{k}}\bigg)\leq\frac{\alpha^{16}}{128n^{8}k^{8}},

(216)

we also have for any
\Delta\geq 0
that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{V_{1:k}}\left[e^{\frac{k}{4}\overline{V}^{2}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{e^{\frac{k}{4}\overline{V}^{2}}>e^{\frac{k}{4}\Delta^{2}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(a)}}{=}e^{\frac{k}{4}\Delta^{2}}{\mathbb{P}}_{V_{1:k}}\left(e^{\frac{k}{4}\overline{V}^{2}}>e^{\frac{k}{4}\Delta^{2}}\right)+\int_{e^{\frac{k}{4}\Delta^{2}}}^{\infty}{\mathbb{P}}_{V_{1:k}}\left(e^{\frac{k}{4}\overline{V}^{2}}>y\right){\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{k\Delta^{2}}{4}}{\mathbb{P}}_{V_{1:k}}\Big(|\overline{V}|>\Delta\Big)+\int_{e^{\frac{k\Delta^{2}}{4}}}^{\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(|\overline{V}|>\sqrt{\frac{4\log y}{k}}\bigg){\textnormal{d}}y

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{k\Delta^{2}}{4}}+\int_{e^{\frac{k\Delta^{2}}{4}}}^{\infty}2e^{-2\log y}{\textnormal{d}}y\leq e^{-\frac{k\Delta^{2}}{4}}-2y^{-1}\Big|_{e^{\frac{k\Delta^{2}}{4}}}^{\infty}=3e^{-\frac{k\Delta^{2}}{4}},

(217)

where (a) follows from Fubini’s formula, namely, for any non-negative random variable
X
with CDF
F_{X}
and any
x\geq 0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[X\mathbbm{1}\{X>x_{0}\}]

\displaystyle=\int_{x_{0}}^{\infty}xF_{X}({\textnormal{d}}x)=\int_{x_{0}}^{\infty}\bigg(\int_{0}^{x}{\textnormal{d}}t\bigg)F_{X}({\textnormal{d}}x)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg(\int_{x_{0}\vee t}^{\infty}F_{X}({\textnormal{d}}x)\bigg){\textnormal{d}}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{x_{0}}{\mathbb{P}}(X>x_{0}){\textnormal{d}}t+\int_{x_{0}}^{\infty}{\mathbb{P}}(X>t){\textnormal{d}}t=x_{0}{\mathbb{P}}(X>x_{0})+\int_{x_{0}}^{\infty}{\mathbb{P}}(X>t){\textnormal{d}}t.

Further, when
\epsilon\leq\frac{1}{64}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha k}{n\log(2nk/\alpha^{2})}}
, one has

2\epsilon^{2}\overline{V}^{2}n\leq\frac{k}{128n}\overline{V}^{2}n\leq\frac{k}{4}\overline{V}^{2}.

Combining this with (
216
) and (
217
) (with
\Delta
set to
\sigma
), and substituting the resulting bounds into (
215
), yields

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 100\epsilon^{2}n\log\Big(\frac{2nk}{\alpha^{2}}\Big)\Big(3+3e^{-\frac{k\sigma^{2}}{4}}\Big)+\frac{\alpha^{16}}{4n^{7}k^{8}}+8\epsilon n{\mathbb{E}}_{V_{1:k}}\Big[e^{\frac{k}{4}\overline{V}^{2}}\mathbbm{1}\{|\overline{V}|>\sigma\}\Big]

(218)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:V_larger_sqrt_k_2}}{\leq}600\epsilon^{2}n\log\Big(\frac{2nk}{\alpha^{2}}\Big)+\frac{\alpha^{16}}{4n^{7}k^{8}}+24\epsilon ne^{-\frac{k\sigma^{2}}{4}}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:def_sigma_tail_thresh}}{\leq}600\epsilon^{2}n\log\Big(\frac{2nk}{\alpha^{2}}\Big)+\frac{\alpha^{16}}{4n^{7}k^{8}}+\frac{\alpha^{8}}{4n^{3}k^{4}}.

Bounding the term
{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
.

We first rewrite
{\mathcal{S}}_{2}
(cf. (
202
)) slightly by using (
201
) as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{2}={\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\Bigg[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})}{F(Y_{1:n},U)}\log\bigg(\frac{F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})}{F(Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg)\bigg]\Bigg]

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:F_n_inverse_upper_bd}}{\leq}{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\Bigg[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg[\frac{F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})}{F(Y_{1:n},U)}\log\bigg(\frac{1}{F(Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg)\bigg]\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\Bigg[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg[\frac{1}{F(Y_{1:n},U)}\log\bigg(\frac{1}{F(Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg)\bigg]\Bigg],

where the last line holds since
\log\Big(\frac{1}{F(Y_{1:n},U)}\Big)\geq 0
.
It can be easily verified that the function
\frac{1}{x}\log\frac{1}{x}
is convex when
0<x\leq 1
. Further, since
F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{v})=\frac{(1+\epsilon\overline{v})^{n}}{e^{\epsilon\overline{v}n}}\leq 1
, one can invoke Jensen’s inequality to obtain

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\left[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg[\frac{1}{F(Y_{1:n},U)}\log\bigg(\frac{1}{F(Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg)\bigg]\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\left[\frac{1}{F(Y_{1:n},U)}\log\bigg(\frac{1}{F(Y_{1:n},U)}\bigg)\right]\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{Y_{1:n},U}\left[\mathop{{\mathbb{E}}}_{V_{1:k}\sim p(\cdot|Y_{1:n},U)}\left[\frac{1}{F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})}\log\frac{1}{F_{n}(\epsilon\overline{V})}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{V_{1:k}}\left[\left(2\big(\epsilon\overline{V}\big)^{2}n\right)\exp\left\{2\big(\epsilon\overline{V}\big)^{2}n\right\}\right],

where the last inequality follows from (
201
), together with the fact that the function

x\mapsto x\log x
is monotonically increasing on
[1,\infty)
.
By letting
\Delta
in (
217
) be
0
, we arrive at

{\mathcal{S}}_{2}\leq 2\epsilon^{2}n{\mathbb{E}}_{V_{1:k}}\Big[e^{\frac{k}{4}\overline{V}^{2}}\Big]\leq 6\epsilon^{2}n.

(219)

Putting all this together.

Finally, combining (
205
), (
218
) and (
219
) with (
202
) yields

2{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sum\limits_{{j}={1}}^{k}(2q_{j}-1)^{2}\bigg]\leq{\mathcal{S}}_{0}+{\mathcal{S}}_{1}+{\mathcal{S}}_{2}\leq 642n\epsilon^{2}\log\Big(\frac{2nk}{\alpha^{2}}\Big)+\frac{\alpha^{6}}{16n^{3}k^{3}},

thereby concluding the proof of Lemma
C.10
.

C.5
Proof of
Proposition

4.2
and
Proposition

4.3

C.5.1
Proof of
Proposition

4.2

Denote
p_{\mathcal{D}}(\cdot)
as the density function of the distribution
{\mathcal{D}}
. According to the definition of Riemann-integrability, for every
\varepsilon>0
, the following holds for large enough
n
:

\displaystyle\bigg|\,{\mathbb{P}}(Y\in{\mathcal{C}}_{n})-(1-\alpha)\,\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\,{\mathbb{P}}(Y\in{\mathcal{C}}_{n})-(1-\alpha)\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}p_{{\mathcal{D}}}(i/n)\,\bigg|+(1-\alpha)\bigg|\,\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\int_{i/n}^{(i+1)/n}\big(p_{{\mathcal{D}}}(i/n)-p_{{\mathcal{D}}}(y)\big){\textnormal{d}}y\,\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\,\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}{\mathbb{P}}\Big(Y\in\big[i/n,\big(i+(1-\alpha)\big)\big/n]\Big)-(1-\alpha)\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}p_{{\mathcal{D}}}(i/n)\,\bigg|+\varepsilon

\displaystyle=\bigg|\,\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\int_{i/n}^{(i+(1-\alpha))/n}\big(p_{\mathcal{D}}(y)-p_{{\mathcal{D}}}(i/n)\big){\textnormal{d}}y\,\bigg|+\varepsilon\leq(2-\alpha)\varepsilon.

This immediately establishes the result of
Proposition

4.2
.

C.5.2
Proof of
Proposition

4.3

The claim follows immediately by invoking
Lemma

C.6
with

k\coloneqq\frac{256K}{\alpha}\quad\text{and}\quad\epsilon\coloneqq\min\Biggl\{\frac{\alpha^{5/2}}{200},\ \frac{1}{64}\sqrt{\frac{\alpha k}{n\log\!\bigl(2nk/\alpha^{2}\bigr)}}\Biggr\}.

Appendix D
Examples of stable learning algorithms

To illustrate the applicability of Assumption
4.3
, this section verifies it for several learning algorithms commonly used in statistical applications.
In particular, Section
D.3
describes how to incorporate stochastic optimization methods into our online conformal framework: the fitted model can be updated incrementally using the newly arrived data at each time step, without retraining from scratch.

D.1
Constrained M-estimation

We begin with the classical constrained M-estimator
(
74
)
that minimizes the empirical loss:

\widehat{\vartheta}_{n}=\mathop{\rm argmin}_{\vartheta\in{\mathcal{C}}}\widehat{L}_{n}(\vartheta)\coloneqq\mathop{\rm argmin}_{\vartheta\in{\mathcal{C}}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell(\vartheta;Z_{i}),

where
\{Z_{i}\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d_{Z}}
denote
n
independent data samples with
Z_{i}
drawn from the distribution
\mathcal{D}_{i}
,

{\mathcal{C}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
represents a closed convex constraint set, and
\ell(\cdot;z)
is a loss metric assumed to be differentiable in
\vartheta
for every
z
.
We also introduce the population
risk
